Coming home on this particular evening, he began to grumble because he found neither light nor supper awaiting him. It must be admitted that there was some justification; for, after all, he was a working-man, and week after week he would return from six days of toil to find a house as dark and squalid as a beggar’s hovel.

“Eh! eh!” he said, as he began to unharness his horse. “This might as well be Isidoro Pane’s shanty! Let us have some light, at any rate, so we can see to swear. What is there for supper?”

“Bacon and eggs; there now, be patient,” said Aunt Martina. “Did you know that Costantino Ledda had been sentenced to thirty years?”

“Twenty-seven. Well, are those the eggs? My dear mamma, that bacon is rancid. Why don’t you give it to the chickens? the chickens, do you hear?” and he snapped his handsome teeth angrily.

===Chinese===
第二天傍晚是个星期六。布龙图·德亚斯（Brontu Dejas）从羊圈回来，人刚跨下马背，就开始抱怨起来。德亚斯一家人关起门来是出了名的爱发牢骚，对外人却总是极其和颜悦色。撇开这一条不谈，他倒是个好性儿的家伙：年轻英俊，肤色黝黑，身形精瘦，中等个子，留着一把卷曲的短期红胡子。他有一口漂亮的牙齿，跟女人们说话时总是笑个不停，好把牙露出来给人看。这天傍晚回到家，他又抱怨上了，因为等着他的既没有灯火，也没有晚饭。平心而论，这里头多少有些道理：他到底是个干力气活的人，一个星期又一个星期，满六天劳碌回来，迎来的却总是一个黑咕隆咚、腌臜不堪的家，跟叫花子栖身的窝棚没有两样。

“哎！哎！”他一面动手给马卸套，一面说，“这地方简直快赶上伊西多罗·帕内（Isidoro Pane）大叔的窝棚了！好歹点个亮吧，好歹看得见骂街。晚饭有什么吃的？”

“腊肉炒鸡蛋；好了好了，耐着点儿性子，”马丁娜婶婶（Aunt Martina）说。“你可知道科斯坦蒂诺·莱达（Costantino Ledda）判了三十年？”

“二十七年。哼，这些就是鸡蛋？我亲爱的妈妈，那块腊肉都哈喇了。怎么不拿去喂鸡？喂鸡，听见没有？”说着，他气得那口漂亮牙齿咬得咔哒咔哒响。

===Original===
“They won’t eat it,” answered Aunt Martina tranquilly. “Yes, twenty-seven. Ah! twenty-seven years, that is a long time. I dreamed he had got penal servitude.”

“Have you been to see the women yet? How pleased they must be now with their fine marriage! Miserable beggars!”

He had asked the question with evident curiosity, yet the moment his mother told him that she had been, and that Giovanna was tearing her hair and quite beside herself, while it was plain to see that Aunt Bachissia wished now that she had strangled her daughter before allowing her to make such a match, he turned on her furiously.

“What business had you to go near the den of those wretched beggars?”

===Chinese===
“鸡不吃，”马丁娜婶婶不动声色地答道。“对，二十七年。唉！二十七年哪，可是段漫长的日子。我还梦见他判了苦役呢。”

“你去看过那两个女人了吗？她们如今对那门好亲事该多称心哪！倒了大霉的穷要饭的！”

他问这话时分明带着好奇；可等母亲告诉他，说她去过了，说乔凡娜正揪着头发、整个人都失了魂，还说巴奇西娅婶婶那副神气，分明是懊悔当初没在让女儿攀这门亲事之前先把她掐死——他一听，立刻翻脸，冲着母亲大发雷霆。

“你跑到那伙穷要饭的狗窝跟前去干什么？”

===Original===
“Ah! my son. Christian charity! You don’t seem to have any idea of what that is!” Aunt Martina liked, indeed, to pretend that she was a charitable person. “Priest Elias was there too this morning; yes, he went to comfort them. Giovanna wants to take the baby to Nuoro for Costantino to see before they carry him off. I told her she was crazy to think of such a thing in this heat; but Priest Elias told her to go, and he nearly cried!”

“What does he know about children! He is barren, like all the rest of them,” snarled Brontu, who hated the priests because his uncle, who had been rector in the village before Priest Elias Portolu came from Nuoro, had left all his property to a hospital. Aunt Martina had not forgiven this outrage either, but the old she-wolf knew how to disguise her feelings, and when Brontu railed against the priests she always made the sign of the cross.

“What makes you talk that way, you fool?” said she, hastily crossing herself. “You don’t know where your feet may carry you! Priest Elias is a saint. If he were to hear such evil talk as that﻿—beware! He has the Holy Books, and if he chooses to, he can curse our fields, and bring the locusts, and make the bees die!”

“A fine saint!” exclaimed Brontu. Then he insisted upon hearing all the particulars about the Eras﻿—how Giovanna had cried out, what that old kite, Aunt Bachissia, had said﻿—

===Chinese===
“哎呀！我的儿。基督徒的仁爱！你好像压根儿不懂那是个什么东西！”马丁娜婶婶确实喜欢装出一副乐善好施的面目。“埃利亚斯神父（Priest Elias）今天早上也去了；是啊，他去安慰她们来着。乔凡娜想抱着孩子上努奥罗去，趁他们还没把科斯坦蒂诺押走，让他看上一眼。我说这么热的天还惦记这种事，她准是疯了；可埃利亚斯神父倒叫她去，他自己差点儿都哭了！”

“他懂什么孩子！他自己就是个不生不养的，他们那号人个个都这样，”布龙图咆哮道。他恨神父，因为他那位叔叔——在埃利亚斯·波尔托卢（Elias Portolu）神父从努奥罗来之前，曾是村里的本堂神父——死后把全部家产都留给了一家医院。马丁娜婶婶对这桩大逆不道的事也同样一直没能饶恕，可这头老母狼懂得遮掩自己的心绪，每逢布龙图破口骂神父，她总要在胸前画个十字。

“你怎么这么说话，你这傻子？”她一边说，一边慌忙画了个十字。“你可不知道自己这两只脚会把你带到哪儿去！埃利亚斯神父是位圣人。他要是听见这种混账话——当心！他手上有圣书，他只要愿意，就能诅咒我们的田地，招来蝗灾，叫蜜蜂死绝！”

“好一位圣人！”布龙图嚷道。接着他偏要人把埃拉家的种种细节讲给他听——乔凡娜是怎么哭喊的，那只老鸢巴奇西娅婶婶又说了些什么——

===Original===
“Well, Giovanna’s sobs were enough to melt the very stones; and Aunt Bachissia was in despair because now, in addition to all the rest, the lawyer’s fees and other expenses of the trial have stripped them of everything they possessed, even to the house.”

The young man listened intently, his face beaming with satisfaction, and his white teeth gleaming. In his undisguised pleasure he was simply and purely savage.

“Listen,” said Aunt Martina, when she had finished. “Giacobbe Dejas will be here presently to see you too. He wanted to begin his term of service tomorrow, but I told him to wait till Monday. Tomorrow is a holiday, and there is no sense in our having him eat at our expense.”

===Chinese===
“唉，乔凡娜哭得呀，连石头听了也要软了；巴奇西娅婶婶绝望透顶，因为如今祸上加祸，律师费加上打官司的种种花销，把他们刮得倾家荡产，连房子也没能剩下。”

年轻人听得全神贯注，满面得意，一口白牙闪闪发亮。他那毫不遮掩的快活劲儿，简直是一种赤裸裸的野蛮。

“你听着，”马丁娜婶婶讲完了，说道。“贾科贝·德亚斯（Giacobbe Dejas）一会儿也要上这儿来见你。他本想明天就开始上工，我叫他等到星期一。明天是个节日，犯不上白叫他来吃我们一天的饭食。”

===Original===
“Beautiful St. Costantino! You *are* close, mamma.”

“Oh, you; you are just like a child! What use is there in wasting things? Life is long and it takes a great deal to live.”

“And how are those two women going to live?” asked Brontu after a short silence, seating himself before the eggs and bread.

“They will catch snails, I suppose,” said Aunt Martina scornfully. She had taken up her spindle again, and was spinning close to the open door. “You take a great interest in them, Brontu Dejas.”

===Chinese===
“好一位圣科斯坦蒂诺！你可*真是*小气，妈妈。”

“嗐，说你呢；你简直像个孩子！糟蹋东西图什么？日子长着呢，过活要用的东西多着呢。”

“那两个女人往后可怎么过活？”沉默了片刻，布龙图在鸡蛋和面包跟前坐下来，问道。

“我看哪，就靠捉蜗牛过活呗，”马丁娜婶婶轻蔑地说。她又拿起纺锤，凑在敞开的门口纺起线来。“你对她们倒真是上心呐，布龙图·德亚斯。”

===Original===
Silence. Within the room the only sounds were the rattle of the spindle and the noise of Brontu’s strong teeth, as he munched the hunks of hard bread; outside, though, beyond the portico, the crickets were chirping incessantly; and from the far-away, deserted woods, through the warm, dim atmosphere of the falling night, came the melancholy cry of an owl. Brontu poured out some wine, raised the glass, and opened his mouth, but not to drink. There was something he wanted to say to his mother, but the words would not come. He drank the wine, brushed some drops off his beard with the back of his hand, and again opened his mouth, but still the words died away.

A sound of heavy boots was heard, tramping across the open space before the house. Aunt Martina, still spinning, arose, told her son that Giacobbe Dejas was coming, and, taking the food and wine, put them away in the cupboard.

Giacobbe saw the action as he entered, and at once understood that she was hiding something in order not to have to offer it to him; but, as he himself would have put it, he was too much a “man of the world” to allow any expression of resentment to escape him.

He advanced, therefore, smiling and cheerful.

===Chinese===
一片寂静。屋里只听得见纺锤的嗡响，和布龙图那口结实牙齿大口嚼着硬面包块的咯吱声；屋外，门廊那头，蟋蟀叫个不停；而从远处荒无人迹的山林里，透过夜幕初降时温热而朦胧的空气，传来猫头鹰凄凉的啼声。布龙图斟了一杯酒，举起杯，张了张嘴，却不是为了喝酒。他有话想对母亲说，可那话就是出不了口。他把酒喝了，用手背抹去胡子上沾的几滴，又张了张嘴，话还是咽了回去。

屋前空地上响起一阵沉重的靴子声。马丁娜婶婶一边纺线一边站起身，告诉儿子贾科贝·德亚斯来了，然后把吃食和酒一并收进碗柜里去。

贾科贝进门时看见了这一手，立刻明白她是把东西藏了起来，免得拿出来招待他；可是，用他自己的话说，他是个太“见过世面”的人，决计不肯让一丝怨气从脸上漏出来。

于是他满脸堆笑、高高兴兴地走上前来。

===Original===
“I will wager,” said he, laying one finger on his nose, “that you were talking about me.”

“No, we were speaking of poor Costantino Ledda.”

“Ah, yes, poor fellow!” returned Giacobbe, becoming serious at once. “And when you think that he is innocent! As innocent as the sun! No one can be more sure of it than I.”

Brontu threw himself back in an easy attitude, crossed his legs, and, turning slightly around, showed his teeth as he did when talking to women. “As to that, opinions may differ,” he said sharply. “There, for instance, is my mother; she dreamed that he had got the death sentence.”

===Chinese===
“我敢打赌，”他说着，一根手指按在鼻子上，“你们方才正在讲我呢。”

“没有，我们在说可怜的科斯坦蒂诺·莱达。”

“啊，是啊，可怜的人！”贾科贝应道，脸色顿时郑重起来。“想想看，他分明是无辜的！清白得跟太阳一样！这一点谁也不会比我更有数。”

布龙图往椅背上一仰，摆出一副闲适的架势，跷起二郎腿，微微侧过身来，露出那口牙齿——跟他对女人们说话时一个样。“这个嘛，各人看法不同，”他尖刻地说。“喏，比方说，我妈就梦见他判了死刑。”

===Original===
“Oh, no, Brontu! What are you talking about? Penal servitude!”

“Well, it amounts to the same thing. Now, we will talk business.”

“Very well, let us talk business, by all means,” assented Giacobbe, crossing his legs as well.

A little later the two men, having settled the matter in hand, went off together, Brontu leading the way to the tavern. He himself was not in the least close, and if he never offered a visitor a glass in his own house, it was only not to irritate Aunt Martina. At the tavern, though, he was superb, and on this particular evening he made Giacobbe drink so much, and drank so much himself, that they both became tipsy.

===Chinese===
“嗳，不，布龙图！你说到哪儿去了？是苦役！”

“得了，反正是一回事。好了，咱们谈正事吧。”

“很好，正事就正事，务必要谈，”贾科贝表示赞成，也跷起了二郎腿。

过了一会儿，两人把手头的事谈妥，便一道出了门，布龙图领着头往酒馆去。他自己手头一点也不紧，只是在自己家里从来不请客人喝一杯，那也无非是不想去招马丁娜婶婶不痛快。可到了酒馆里，他出手就阔气极了；这天晚上，他劝贾科贝喝了一杯又一杯，自己也一杯接一杯地灌，结果两个人都喝得醉醺醺的。

===Original===
Coming out at last into the silent, deserted street, filled with the odour of the dry fields, they began talking again of Costantino, and Brontu said, with brutal frankness, that he was glad of the sentence.

“Go to the devil!” shouted Giacobbe. “You have no heart!”

“All right, that’s it; I have no heart.”

“Just because Giovanna wouldn’t have you, you are glad to hear of the death, or worse than death, of a brother.”

===Chinese===
两人终于出了酒馆，来到寂静无人的街上；街上弥漫着干燥田野的气味。他们又谈起科斯坦蒂诺来，布龙图带着一种粗鲁的直白说，判了这个刑，他很高兴。

“见你的鬼去吧！”贾科贝嚷道。“你这个人没有心肝！”

“不错，正是这样；我就是没有心肝。”

“就因为乔凡娜不肯要你，你听见一个弟兄遭了死劫——遭了比死还惨的下场——就乐成这样。”

===Original===
“He’s not dead, and he’s not a brother; and it was I who would not have Giovanna Era. If I had wanted her to, she would have licked the soles of my shoes.”

“Bum﻿—bum﻿—look out, or you’ll have a tumble, my little spring bird. You lie like a servant-maid.”

“I﻿—I﻿—am﻿—not﻿—a﻿—a﻿—servant-maid,” stammered Brontu, furious. “If you say anything like that again, I’ll take you by the crown of your head and choke you.”

“Bum﻿—I tell you, you’ll fall down, little spring bird,” repeated Giacobbe at the top of his lungs. Their voices rang out through the quiet street; then they suddenly ceased talking, and stillness reigned once more. In the distance, under the light of the stars which overhung the mountain crests like garlands of golden flowers, the owl still sounded his melancholy note.

===Chinese===
“他没死，他也不是什么弟兄；况且，是我不肯要乔凡娜·埃拉。当年只要我肯要她，她早把我的鞋底舔了。”

“嘣——嘣——留神，要栽跟头喽，我的小春鸟。你撒起谎来活脱脱是个女仆。”

“我——我——才——不——是——女、女仆，”布龙图结结巴巴地说，气得要命。“你再说一句这样的话，我就揪住你的天灵盖，掐死你。”

“嘣——我告诉你，你要摔跟头的，小春鸟，”贾科贝扯足了嗓门重复道。两人的声音在静静的街上回荡；随后他们忽然住了口，四下重又归于寂静。远处，在像金色花朵编成的花环一般低垂于群山峰顶的星光下，那只猫头鹰还在一声声叫着它的哀音。

===Original===
All at once Brontu began to cry in a strange, drunken fashion, with neither sobs nor tears.

“Well, what is the matter now?” demanded Giacobbe in a low tone. “Are you drunk?”

“Yes, I am. Drunk with poison, you galley refuse. I only hope you will be strangled yet!”

At this the other felt very indignant. Not only had he never been to prison, but he had never so much as been accused of any offence against the law. Yet, mingled with his resentment, there was a vague feeling of terror.

===Chinese===
忽然间，布龙图用一种醉酒人特有的怪样子哭了起来，既不抽噎，也没有眼泪。

“喂，这又是怎么了？”贾科贝低声问道。“你醉了？”

“对了，我醉了。中了毒似的醉了，你这苦役坯子。我只巴望你早晚也让人勒死！”

这话叫那一个十分气恼。他非但从没蹲过监狱，连违犯法律的罪名都不曾沾过一回。可是怨愤之余，他心里又升起一阵说不清道不明的恐惧。

===Original===
“You are going crazy!” said he in a still lower tone. “What’s the matter with you? Why should you talk to me like that? Have I ever done anything to you?”

Whereupon the other became confidential, and, groaning as though he were in physical pain, he declared that he was, in truth, madly in love with Giovanna, and that he had hoped, and prayed the devil, from the beginning, that Costantino would be found guilty.

“Even if the devil were to get my soul it wouldn’t matter, because, you see, I don’t believe in him!” said he, breaking into a foolish, cackling laugh, more disagreeable to listen to even than his previous maudlin distress. “I intend to marry Giovanna,” he presently added.

Giacobbe was greatly astonished at this, but he pretended to be still more so. “What!” said he. “You take my breath away! How﻿—why﻿—what on earth do you mean? How can you marry her?”

===Chinese===
“你怕是要疯了！”他把声音压得更低，说道。“你怎么了？凭什么这么跟我说话？我几时亏待过你？”

于是那一个推心置腹起来。他呻吟着，像是身上疼得厉害，供认说自己实在是疯了似的恋着乔凡娜，还说他打从一开始就巴望着、向魔鬼祈求着，让科斯坦蒂诺被判有罪。

“就算魔鬼真要把我的灵魂收去，也无所谓，因为，你瞧，我压根儿不信有他！”他说着，迸出一阵又蠢又刺耳的咯咯笑声，比先前那副醉态百出的伤感还叫人听着难受。“我打算娶乔凡娜，”过了一会儿他又补上一句。

贾科贝听了大吃一惊，可他偏装出比这还吃惊十倍的样子。“什么！”他说。“你这话惊得我喘不上气来！怎么——为什么——你到底打的什么主意？你怎么娶得了她？”

===Original===
“She will get a divorce, that’s all. Well, what of that? There’s a law that gives a woman the right to marry again if her husband has been sent to prison for a long sentence.”

Giacobbe had heard some talk of this, but no case of legal divorce, still less of re-marriage, had as yet been heard of in Orlei. Nevertheless, not to appear ignorant, he said: “Oh, yes, I know; but it is a mortal sin. Giovanna Era will never do it!”

“That’s just what I am worrying about, Giacobbe Dejas. Will you talk to her on the subject tomorrow?”

“Oh, yes, of course! Tomorrow! You’re an ass, Brontu Dejas! You may be rich, but you are as stupid as a lizard, stupider than one! Here, when you might marry a maid﻿—some rich young girl, as fresh as a rose with the dew still on it﻿—you want instead to have that woman! Upon my word, it will give me something to laugh at for the next seven months!”

===Chinese===
“她会离婚的，就这么回事。那又怎么样？有条法律，做丈夫的要是判了长期徒刑关进大牢，做妻子的就有权再嫁。”

这样的事贾科贝也风闻过一点，可是在奥莱伊（Orlei），合法离婚的事还从来没听说过，离婚再嫁就更没有影儿了。不过，为了不显得自己孤陋寡闻，他说：“噢，是啊，我知道；可那是死罪。乔凡娜·埃拉决不会干的！”

“我操心的正是这个，贾科贝·德亚斯。你明天肯不肯替我去跟她提一提这桩事？”

“噢，好啊，一定去！明天！你真是头蠢驴，布龙图·德亚斯！你兴许有钱，可你蠢得像只蜥蜴，比蜥蜴还蠢！你放着好好的姑娘不娶——哪个体面人家的年轻姑娘，像朵露水还没干的玫瑰那么水灵——偏偏要那个女人！说真的，往后七个月我可有的笑啦！”

===Original===
“All right, you can laugh till you split in two, like a ripe pomegranate! But I’m going to marry her!” said Brontu angrily. “There’s no other woman like her, and I shall marry her; you will see!”

“Well, do marry her, my little spring bird!” cried the other, bursting into a loud laugh. Brontu joined in, and they continued on their way uproariously till they saw a tall figure with a staff silently approaching them.

“Uncle Isidoro Pane, did you have good sport?” shouted Giacobbe. “And your legs, have they plenty of punctures?”

“You had better turn leech-fisher yourself,” said the other, coming up to them. “Whew! what a smell of brandy! Someone must have broken a cask near here!”

===Chinese===
“行啊，你尽管笑，笑得像只熟透的石榴一样炸成两半！反正我要娶她！”布龙图怒气冲冲地说。“天底下再没有第二个像她那样的女人了，我一定要娶她；你走着瞧！”

“好，那就娶她吧，我的小春鸟！”那一个放声大笑，喊着说道。布龙图也跟着大笑，两人一路喧喧嚷嚷地往前走，直到看见一个拄着杖的高高身影，一声不响地朝他们迎面走来。

“伊西多罗·帕内大叔，收获不坏吧？”贾科贝喊道。“你那两条腿呢，叫蚂蟥叮出好些窟窿眼儿了吧？”

“你倒不如自己也去捕蚂蟥得了，”那一位走上前来，说道。“嚯！好冲的白兰地酒气！准是有人在这附近打破了酒桶！”

===Original===
“Do you mean that you think we are drunk?” demanded Brontu in a bullying tone. “The only reason you don’t get drunk yourself is because you haven’t anything to do it with! Get away! get away, I tell you, or I’ll crush you like a frog!”

The old man laughed softly, and walked on.

“Idiot!” said Giacobbe in an undertone. “Don’t you know that he could have helped you with Giovanna? He’s a friend of hers.”

“Here! here!” shouted Brontu, turning around, and gesticulating with both arms. “Come back! come back, I tell you! ‘Sidore Pane, *che ti morsichi il cane*!’ ”4 he laughed, delighted with his rhyme. But Isidoro did not stop.

===Chinese===
“你是说，觉得我们醉了？”布龙图气势汹汹地质问道。“你自己不喝醉，无非是因为你手里没有能醉人的东西！滚开！给我滚开，不然我就把你像只青蛙一样碾扁！”

老头儿轻轻地笑了，继续走他的路。

“傻瓜！”贾科贝压低了嗓音说。“你难道不知道，他本可以在乔凡娜的事上帮你一把？他是她的朋友。”

“这儿！这儿！”布龙图喊道，转过身去，双臂乱挥。“回来！我叫你回来！‘西多雷·帕内，*che ti morsichi il cane*！’ ”4他哈哈大笑，为自己的韵脚得意得很。可伊西多罗还是没有停下。

===Original===
“Do you hear me?” yelled the tipsy Brontu, stammering somewhat. “I tell you to come here! Ah! you won’t do it, you little toad? I tell﻿—you﻿—”

But Isidoro silently pursued his way.

“Don’t talk to him like that; what sort of way is this to carry on?” remonstrated Giacobbe. Brontu thereupon adopted a new method.

“Little flower, come here, come here! Come listen to what I have to say. You may tell *her*﻿—that friend of yours﻿—well, yes, Giovanna, that is who I mean. You may tell her that if she gets a divorce I’ll marry her!”

===Chinese===
“你听见没有？”醉醺醺的布龙图吼道，说话有些结巴。“我叫你到这儿来！啊！你不肯，你这小癞蛤蟆？我叫——你——”

可伊西多罗只管默默地走自己的路。

“别那样跟他说话；哪有这么跟人纠缠的？”贾科贝责备道。布龙图于是换了个法子。

“小花儿，过来，过来！来听听我要说什么话。你不妨告诉*她*——你那位朋友——对，就是乔凡娜，我说的是她。你可以告诉她，只要她离了婚，我就娶她！”

===Original===
This had the desired effect. The old man stopped short, and turning around, called in a distinct voice:

“Giacobbe Dejas!”

“What is it, my dear?” answered the herdsman mockingly.

“Make﻿—him﻿—keep﻿—quiet!” returned Isidoro in the tone of a person who means to be obeyed.

===Chinese===
这话立刻见了效。老头儿猛地站住，转过身来，用清清楚楚的声音喊道：

“贾科贝·德亚斯！”

“什么事，亲爱的？”牧人嘲弄地应道。

“让——他——住——嘴！”伊西多罗答道，那口气是个说一不二、惯于叫人服从的人的口气。

===Original===
For some unexplained reason, Giacobbe felt a sudden sense of chill as he heard the tone and those four emphatic words. Taking his new master by the arm he drew him quickly away, murmuring:

“You are a dunce! You behave as though you had no sense at all! What a way to talk!”

“Didn’t you tell me to yourself?”

“I? You are dreaming! Am I crazy?”

===Chinese===
不知为什么，听到那口气和那四个一字一顿的字，贾科贝只觉一阵寒意袭上心头。他拉住他这位新东家的胳膊，赶紧把他拖开，嘴里嘟哝着：

“你可真是个笨蛋！你这做派，好像一点脑子都没有！哪能这么说话！”

“不是你自己跟我说的吗？”

“我？你做梦呢！我疯了不成？”

===Original===
They continued on their way, staggering along together, arm in arm. On the portico they found Aunt Martina, still spinning. She saw at once that her son was tipsy, but said nothing, knowing by experience that to irritate him when he was in that condition was only to arouse him to a state of fury. When he asked for wine, though, she said there was none.

“Ah! there is none? No wine in the Dejas’ house! The richest people in the neighbourhood! What a miserly mother you are.” Then he began to bluster: “I’m not going to make a scandal, but I can tell you I am going to marry Giovanna Era!”

“Yes, yes, you are going to marry her,” said Aunt Martina to quiet him. “But in the meantime, go to bed, and don’t make such a noise; if she hears you, she won’t have you.”

He quieted down, but made Giacobbe unroll a couple of rush mats and spread them on the floor; then, throwing himself down, nothing would do but the herdsman must lie down as well, and sleep beside him; and rather than have any trouble, Aunt Martina was obliged to agree.

Thus it fell out that instead of beginning his term of service on the Monday, Giacobbe entered his new place on Saturday evening.

===Chinese===
两人互相搂着肩膀，踉踉跄跄地继续往回走。到了门廊，他们看见马丁娜婶婶还在纺线。她一眼就看出儿子喝多了，却什么也没说，凭经验她知道，他那个当口去招惹他，只会惹得他勃然大怒。不过等他讨酒喝时，她说家里没有酒了。

“啊！没有了？德亚斯家里没有酒！这方圆左近最有钱的人家！你这当妈的可真抠门儿。”接着他嚷嚷起来：“我不是要闹事，可我把话搁在这儿，我要娶乔凡娜·埃拉！”

“是，是，你要娶她，”马丁娜婶婶这么哄着他。“不过眼下你先上床去，别这么嚷嚷；让她听见了，她可就不肯要你了。”

他安静了下来，却叫贾科贝解开两张灯芯草席，铺在地上；随后往上一躺，又非缠着牧人也躺下、陪他一起睡不可；马丁娜婶婶不想再生事端，只好依了他。

就这样，贾科贝没有等到星期一才上工，星期六晚上就进了新东家的门。

## IX / 第九章

===Original===
Nineteen Hundred and Ten. In the “strangers’ room” of the Porru house, Giovanna was looking over some purchases made that day in Nuoro. She was stouter than ever, and had lost something of her girlish look, but, nevertheless, she was both fresh and handsome still. She examined the pieces of linen and woollen stuff attentively, turning them over and over and feeling them with a preoccupied air, as though not altogether satisfied with the selection; then, folding them carefully, she wrapped them in newspaper and laid them away in her bag.

===Chinese===
一九一〇年。在波鲁家的“客房”里，乔凡娜正在检视当天在努奥罗买下的各色东西。她比先前任何时候都丰满了些，少女的模样已失去了几分，但依然水灵，依然好看。她仔细察看那一匹匹亚麻布和呢料，翻来覆去，用手摩挲，一副心事重重的样子，仿佛对这批选下的货色并不全然满意；随后她把布料仔细叠好，用报纸包上，收进提包里。

===Original===
These things were the materials for her wedding outfit, for, having at last obtained her divorce, she was shortly to marry Dejas. She and her mother had come to Nuoro for the express purpose of making the purchases. The money had been borrowed with the utmost secrecy from Aunt Anna-Rosa Dejas, Giacobbe’s sister, who had always taken a particular interest in Giovanna because of having been for a short time her foster-mother. It was the dead of winter, but the two women had courageously defied the fatigues and discomforts of the journey in order to lay in a supply of linen, cotton, kerchiefs, and woollen stuffs. The ceremony, a purely civil one, was to be conducted in the strictest privacy, more so, even, than on the occasion of a widow’s marriage. But this made no difference to Aunt Bachissia, who was determined that her daughter should enter her new home fitted out in every respect like a youthful bride of good family.

===Chinese===
这些东西，都是她新娘行头的衣料——她终于获准离婚，不久就要嫁给德亚斯了。她和她母亲就是为了采买，专程来的努奥罗。钱是极其秘密地从安娜-罗莎·德亚斯（Anna-Rosa Dejas）婶婶那里借来的——她是贾科贝的妹妹，因为做过乔凡娜短时期的乳母，一向对她格外上心。时值隆冬，母女俩却硬是不顾旅途的劳顿与辛苦，来置办一批亚麻布、棉布、头巾和呢料。婚礼纯是世俗婚礼，要办得比寡妇再嫁还要隐秘。可这在巴奇西娅婶婶看来毫无分别：她拿定主意，女儿进新家门，就得像个好人家的年轻新娘那样，样样齐备、一身光鲜。

===Original===
The countryside was still wondering and gossipping over the scandalous affair, and it was rumoured that another couple contemplated applying for a divorce﻿—by mutual consent. A great many people already looked askance at the Eras, and some said that Brontu had evil designs upon Giovanna. Giacobbe Dejas, Isidoro Pane, and a number of other friends had stopped going to the house after making final scenes that were almost violent. Giacobbe had snarled like a dog, and had used prayers and even threats in a last, vain effort to dissuade Giovanna from the step, until Aunt Bachissia had, at length, driven him out. Even Aunt Porredda at Nuoro, although it was her son who had obtained the divorce for Giovanna, had received her friends with marked coolness. The “Doctor,” as she called her son, was, on the contrary, most cordial and attentive in his manner towards their guests.

===Chinese===
乡间还在为这桩丑事啧啧称奇、议论纷纷，又风传另有一对夫妇打算申请离婚——两相情愿的那种。许多人已经拿白眼看埃拉母女，还有人说布龙图对乔凡娜不怀好意。贾科贝·德亚斯、伊西多罗·帕内和另外一些朋友，在最后那几场几乎动武的吵闹之后，就再不上这家的门了。贾科贝像狗一样龇牙咆哮，又是恳求又是恐吓，作最后一番徒劳的努力想劝乔凡娜罢手，直到巴奇西娅婶婶最后把他轰了出去。就连努奥罗的波蕾达婶婶——虽说替乔凡娜办下这桩离婚的正是她儿子——接待她的朋友们时也透着分外的冷淡。倒是那位“大夫”——她这样称呼自己的儿子——对客人们极其热络殷勤。

===Original===
So Giovanna was folding up her possessions in a thoughtful mood, her preoccupation having, however, to do solely with those bits of stuff. The linen, it appeared, was somewhat tumbled; the fringe of the black Thibet kerchief, with its big crimson roses, was too short; one piece of ribbon had a spot on it﻿—worrying matters, all of them.

===Chinese===
就这样，乔凡娜心事重重地折叠着自己置办的这些东西，不过她的心事仅仅同这几块料子有关。那亚麻布似乎有些皱了；那条缀着大朵深红玫瑰的黑西藏呢头巾，流苏嫌短了；有一段缎子上落了个斑点——桩桩都是叫人烦心的事。

===Original===
Night was falling﻿—like that *other time*﻿—but the surroundings, and the weather, and﻿—her heart, were all, quite, quite different. The “strangers’ room” now had a fine window, through whose panes shone the clear, cold light of a winter evening. The furniture, all entirely new, exhaled a powerful smell of varnished wood, while its surface glistened like hoarfrost. The door opened on the same covered gallery, but new granite steps now led down to the courtyard. The “Doctor’s” practice was growing, and the entire house had been done over. He now had an office in the busiest part of the town, and was much in demand both for civil and penal processes. The most desperate cases, the worst offenders, all that class of clients who have the least to hope from the law, entrusted their affairs to him.

===Chinese===
夜幕正在降临——像*上一次*那样——可是周围的环境、天气，还有——她的心，全都全然不同了。“客房”如今有了一扇漂亮的窗子，冬夜清冷的亮光透过窗玻璃照进来。家具全是崭新的，散发出一股浓烈的上漆木头的气味，表面亮闪闪的像落了层霜花。房门开向那同一条带顶的回廊，但如今是一色新的花岗岩石阶通到院子里。“大夫”的生意蒸蒸日上，整座屋子都翻修过了。他在城里最热闹的地段开了一间事务所，民事案子、刑事案子都忙得不可开交。那些最没指望的案子、最劣迹昭彰的案犯——所有那一类对法律最不敢存指望的主顾——都把身家事务托付给他。

===Original===
Giovanna folded, wrapped, and packed her possessions, and then, the bag being somewhat over-full, she shook it vigorously to make the contents settle down; this accomplished, she turned with knitted brows, and slowly descended the outer stair, both hands thrust deep in the pockets always to be found just below the waist in the skirt of a Sardinian costume.

===Chinese===
乔凡娜叠好、包好、装好自己的东西；提包塞得有点太满，她用力抖了几抖，好让里面的东西沉实下去。弄完这些，她皱着眉转过身，慢慢走下屋外的楼梯，双手深深插在口袋里——撒丁岛裙装的裙腰下方两侧，总有着这样的口袋。

===Original===
It was an evening in January, clear but extremely cold. Some silver stars, set in the cloudless blue of the sky, seemed to tremble in the frosty atmosphere. Crossing the courtyard Giovanna could see, through the window of the lighted dining-room, Grazia’s pale face and great, eager eyes as she sat turning over the leaves of a fashion paper. The child had developed into a tall and pretty girl; she was dressed in the latest fashion, with great lace wings extending from the shoulders behind the arms; they obliged their wearers to walk sideways through any narrow aperture, but made them look, by way of compensation, like so many angels before the fall.

===Chinese===
那是一月里的一个夜晚，天色清朗，却冷得厉害。几颗银色的星嵌在万里无云的碧空里，在凛冽的空气中似乎瑟瑟颤动。乔凡娜穿过院子时，透过饭厅亮着的窗子，望见格拉齐娅苍白的脸和那双急切的大眼睛——她正坐着翻一本时装报纸。这孩子已经出落成一个高挑秀美的姑娘；她穿着最时兴的衣裳，肩头向臂后舒展出巨大的蕾丝翅膀；这翅膀逼得穿的人凡过窄门都得侧着身子走，但作为补偿，也让她们一个个看上去像堕落之前的天使。

===Original===
Grazia, seeing the guest, smiled at her without getting up, and the latter entered the kitchen.

===Chinese===
格拉齐娅看见客人，没起身，只朝她笑了笑；客人便进了厨房。

===Original===
Here, too, everything was new; the white walls, the stove of glistening bricks, the petroleum lamp hanging from the ceiling. It was all so gorgeous that Aunt Bachissia could not refrain from gazing about her the whole time, her shining, little, green beads of eyes, snapping and sparkling in the sallow, hawklike face, set in the folds of a black scarf. She at least, was unchanged﻿—the old witch! She was seated beside the servant-maid, a dirty, dishevelled young person, whose loud and frequent laugh displayed a set of protruding teeth. Aunt Porredda was cooking, and scolding the maid for this annoying habit of hers. Only fancy! Here was the mistress doing the cooking, while the servant sat by the stove and﻿—laughed! What kind of way to do was that? And, moreover, the good woman could never have one single moment’s peace, and she the mother of a famous lawyer!

===Chinese===
这里也样样都是新的：白墙，亮闪闪的砖灶，吊在天花板上的煤油灯。这一切都这样阔气，巴奇西娅婶婶禁不住一直四下张望，她那双亮晶晶的绿珠子般的小眼睛，在黑头巾褶包裹着的蜡黄的、鹰隼似的脸上滴溜溜地闪。至少她是老样子没变——这个老巫婆！她坐在女仆旁边；那是个又脏又头发蓬乱的年轻姑娘，笑起来又响又勤，露出一口龅牙。波蕾达婶婶正在做饭，一面骂女仆这讨厌的毛病。想想看！主人家亲自下厨，女仆倒坐在灶边——笑！天下有这样的道理吗？再说，这位善良的妇人连一刻安宁也捞不着，而她可是位大名鼎鼎的律师的母亲！

===Original===
Giovanna seated herself at some little distance from the stove, stooping over with her hands still buried in the pockets of her skirt.

===Chinese===
乔凡娜在离灶稍远的地方坐下，佝偻着身子，双手仍旧深深埋在裙子的口袋里。

===Original===
“Just look!” exclaimed Aunt Bachissia in a tone of envy. “This kitchen might be a parlour! You must do *your* kitchen up like this, Giovanna.”

===Chinese===
“你瞧瞧！”巴奇西娅婶婶用一种艳羡的口气嚷道，“这厨房简直抵得上客厅！你也得把*你*家的厨房照这样收拾，乔凡娜。”

===Original===
“Yes,” said the young woman absentmindedly.

===Chinese===
“是啊。”少妇心不在焉地应道。

===Original===
“Yes? Well, upon my soul, I should say so! Godmother Malthina is close, but you have got to make her understand that money is meant to spend. A kitchen like this﻿—why, it is heaven﻿—upon my soul! This is living.”

===Chinese===
“是啊？嗐，我拿性命起誓，可不是嘛！马尔蒂娜教母抠是抠，可你得让她明白，钱就是生来花的。这样的厨房——嗐，这是天堂——我拿性命起誓！这才叫过日子。”

===Original===
“What do you always say ‘upon my soul’ for?” asked the giggling servant-maid.

===Chinese===
“您干吗老是说‘我拿性命起誓’呀？”咯咯笑着的贴身女仆问道。

===Original===
“If she doesn’t choose to spend her money, how am I to make her?” said Giovanna with a sigh.

===Chinese===
“她不肯花她的钱，我有什么法子叫她花？”乔凡娜叹口气说。
===Original===
The servant was still laughing, but Aunt Porredda, who wanted to keep out of her guests’ conversation, turned on her, and sharply ordered her to grate some cheese for the macaroni. The girl obeyed.

===Chinese===
女仆还在笑，波蕾达婶婶本不想掺和客人的谈话，便转过身去冲着她，厉声吩咐她擦些干酪下来拌通心粉。姑娘照办了。

===Original===
“What is the matter with you?” asked Aunt Bachissia as Giovanna sighed again.

===Chinese===
“你是怎么了？”乔凡娜又叹气，巴奇西娅婶婶问道。

===Original===
“She remembers!” said Aunt Porredda to herself. “After all, she is a Christian, not an animal, and she can’t help herself!”

===Chinese===
“她想起旧事来了！”波蕾达婶婶心里说，“她到底是个基督徒，不是畜生，由不得自己啊！”

===Original===
But Giovanna spoke up crossly:

===Chinese===
可乔凡娜没好气地开了口：

===Original===
“Well, it’s just this; they’ve cheated us. That is not good linen, and the ribbon is spotted. Oh! it is too much.”

===Chinese===
“嗐，就是这么回事；咱们叫人给坑了。那不是好亚麻布，缎子上还有斑点。哦！真是欺人太甚。”

===Original===
“Upon my soul!” said the maid, mimicking Aunt Bachissia’s voice and accent, and grating away vigorously on the cheese.

===Chinese===
“我拿性命起誓！”女仆学着巴奇西娅婶婶的嗓音和腔调说，手上的干酪擦得起劲。

===Original===
Aunt Porredda thereupon let out upon her all the vials of wrath she would fain have emptied upon her guests, calling her by all the names which, in her secret heart, she was applying to Giovanna﻿—“shameless,” “vile,” “ungrateful,” “despicable,” and so on, and threatening to strike her over the head with the ladle. In her terror, the girl grated the skin off one finger, and she was in the act of displaying it with the blood streaming down when the lawyer-son limped briskly into the room. He was enveloped in a long, black overcoat, so full that it looked like a cloak with sleeves. His smooth, fresh-coloured little face beamed with the self-satisfied expression of a nursing child. Asking immediately what there was to eat, he dropped into a seat beside Aunt Bachissia, and sat there chatting until supper was ready. After him the little Minnia came running in, rosy, breathless, and dishevelled, and threw herself down by the servant-maid. The boy had died three years earlier. The little girl’s dress, of black and red flannel, was pretty enough, but her shoes were torn and her hands dirty. She had spent the entire day tearing around in a neighbouring truck-garden, and began to pour out confidences to the servant in an eager undertone.

===Chinese===
波蕾达婶婶于是把一肚子本想对着客人们发作的雷霆之怒，统统泼到了她身上，用她心底里正用来骂乔凡娜的那些字眼骂她——“不要脸”、“下贱”、“忘恩负义”、“卑鄙”等等，还威胁要用长柄勺敲她的头。姑娘吓慌了，把一根手指的皮都擦掉了；她正举着那根手指、血往下淌给人看的时候，那位当律师的儿子一瘸一拐、步子却轻快地走进屋来。他裹在一件黑色长大衣里，大衣肥大得像件带袖子的斗篷。他那张光滑红润的小脸，放着吃奶娃娃般自满自得的光。一进门就问有什么吃的，随即在巴奇西娅婶婶身边落了座，坐在那里闲谈，一直谈到晚饭备好。他之后，小米妮娅跑了进来，脸蛋红扑扑、气喘吁吁、头发散乱，一屁股坐在女仆身边。那男孩三年前就死了。小姑娘那身红黑法兰绒的衣裳够漂亮了，鞋却破着，手也脏着。她一整天都在邻近的菜园里疯跑，这时开始急急地低声向女仆倾吐心事。

===Original===
“Upon my soul!” repeated the servant, in the same tone as before.

===Chinese===
“我拿性命起誓！”女仆又说了一遍，还是先前那个腔调。

===Original===
Next Uncle Efes Maria’s big face, with its thick, wide-open lips, appeared in the door, wanting to know why they could not have supper right away.

===Chinese===
接着，埃费斯·马里亚大叔那张咧着厚嘴唇的大脸在门口探了进来，要问问为什么还不能马上开饭。

===Original===
The dining-room was now furnished with two tall, shining cupboards of varnished wood, and the whole apartment had quite an air of elegance﻿—strips of carpet on the stone floor, a stove, and so on. Poor Aunt Porredda, with her big feet and hobnailed shoes, never felt really at home there; while Uncle Efes Maria had not yet cured himself of the habit of staring proudly around him. Grazia, tall and elegant, always withdrew into herself when her relations came into this room, where she passed most of her time eagerly devouring the *Unique Mode*, the *Petite Parisienne*, and the fashion articles of a family journal﻿—sufficiently immoral in its tone, since it fomented such unhealthy dreams in her foolish head. Ah, those low-cut gowns, covered with embroidery; those scarfs worked in gold; those bodices with their great wings of silver lace, the rainbow hues, the spangles glittering like frost! Ah, those hats covered with artificial fruits, and the long flower boas, and petticoats trimmed with lace at thirty lire a yard, and the painted gloves, and fans made of human skin! How beautiful it all was﻿—horribly, terrifyingly beautiful! Merely to read about these things gave her a sort of spasm, they were so beautiful, so beautiful, so beautiful. And afterwards, how ugly and common and flat everything seemed﻿—the simple old grandmother, with her fat, wrinkled face; and the dull grandfather, gazing about him with such ignorant satisfaction and pride! It was all simply stultifying.

===Chinese===
饭厅如今摆着两只亮闪闪的上漆大木柜，整套屋子颇有几分雅致气派——石板地上铺着地毯条，还有火炉，等等。可怜的波蕾达婶婶，凭她那双大脚和钉着平头钉的鞋，在这里从来不曾真正觉得自在；埃费斯·马里亚大叔呢，也还没改掉那副得意洋洋四下打量的习惯。高雅颀长的格拉齐娅，每当亲戚们走进这间屋子，总是敛缩回自己的天地里——她一天里大部分光阴都泡在这里，如饥似渴地吞咽着《*Unique Mode*》、《*Petite Parisienne*》，还有一份家庭杂志的时装栏——那份杂志的格调足够伤风败俗，足以在她那颗痴脑袋里煽起种种病态的梦想。啊，那些绣满花样的低领长裙；那些织金的披巾；那些镶着银蕾丝大翅膀的紧身胸衣，还有那彩虹般的色彩、霜花般闪烁的亮片！啊，那些缀满假水果的帽子，长长的花串围脖，镶着一码三十里拉的蕾丝花边的衬裙，还有彩绘手套、人皮做的扇子！这一切多么美啊——美得可怕，美得吓人！单是读到这些东西，就叫她一阵阵心口发紧，它们太美了，太美了，太美了。而读过之后，一切又显得多么丑陋、平庸、乏味——朴实的祖母，胖胖的一张皱纹脸；迟钝的祖父，带着那样懵懂的自得与骄傲四下张望！这一切简直叫人变傻。

===Original===
Just as on that other, faraway evening, Aunt Porredda came in, bearing triumphantly the steaming dish of macaroni, and all the members of the party seated themselves around the table. Aunt Bachissia, finding herself in the shadow, so to speak, of Grazia’s wings, forthwith broke anew into loud exclamations of wonder and admiration, this time apropos of those glorious objects:

===Chinese===
正像那个遥远的傍晚一样，波蕾达婶婶端着热气腾腾的一大盘通心粉，得意洋洋地走了进来，一行人都围着桌子坐下。巴奇西娅婶婶发觉自己——可以这么说——正处在格拉齐娅那对翅膀的阴影之下，立刻又高声惊叹、赞美起来，这一回是冲着那些光彩夺目的物事：

===Original===
“No, we have never seen anything like that in our neighbourhood, but then, we have no ladies there. Here they all look like angels, the ladies.”

===Chinese===
“不，我们那地方从来没见过这样的东西；不过话说回来，我们那儿也没有太太小姐。这儿呢，太太们个个都像天使。”

===Original===
“Or bats,” said Uncle Efes Maria. “Eh, it’s the fashion, my dears. Why, I remember when I was a child the ladies were all big and round; they looked like cupolas. There hardly were any ladies in those days﻿—the Superintendent’s wife, the family﻿—”

===Chinese===
“要不就是蝙蝠。”埃费斯·马里亚大叔说，“嗐，这就叫时兴啊，孩子们。我小时候，太太们个个又高又圆，活像一个个圆屋顶。那年头简直数不出几位太太来——省督夫人，还有她一家子——”

===Original===
“And then that thing behind,” interrupted Aunt Porredda. “Oh! I remember that, it looked like a saddle. Well, if you’ll believe me, upon my word and honour, I remember one time someone sat down on one of them.”

===Chinese===
“还有后腰上那个玩意儿。”波蕾达婶婶插嘴道，“哦！那个我记得，看上去像一副马鞍。嘿，你们信不信，我凭名誉起誓，我记得有一回，有人一屁股坐在那上头了。”

===Original===
“The last time we were here,” said Aunt Bachissia, “those wings were little things; now they are growing, growing.”

===Chinese===
“上回我们来的时候，”巴奇西娅婶婶说，“这些翅膀还是些小玩意儿；如今越长越大了。”
===Original===
Grazia sat eating her supper as though she did not hear a word of what the others were saying. The “Doctor” eat his too﻿—like a gristmill﻿—staring at his niece all the while with the look of a pleased child. “Growing, growing,” said he. “The next thing we know they’ll all take flight.”

===Chinese===
格拉齐娅坐着吃她的晚饭，仿佛别人说的一个字也没听进。“大夫”也吃他的——像座磨盘似的——一面老用孩子得偿所愿般的眼神盯着他的外甥女。“越长越大，越长越大，”他说，“再往后，小心她们一个个都飞起来。”

===Original===
Grazia shrugged her shoulders, or rather her wings, and neither spoke nor looked up. She frequently found her uncle﻿—that hero of her first, young dream﻿—very trying, and worse than trying﻿—foolish! It was the common talk of the town that the uncle and niece were going to marry, and he, when interrogated on the subject, would answer neither yes nor no.

===Chinese===
格拉齐娅耸了耸肩——不如说耸了耸她的翅膀——既不说话，也不抬头。她常常觉得她这位舅舅——她少女时代最初梦里的那位英雄——实在叫人难堪，比难堪更糟——愚蠢！城里纷纷传说舅舅要娶外甥女，而他，被问到这件事时，既不说是，也不说不是。

===Original===
The conversation continued for some time on impersonal topics. Every now and then Aunt Porredda would get up and pass in and out of the room, and occasionally the talk would die away, and long pauses ensue that were almost embarrassing. Like that *other time* everyone instinctively avoided the subject uppermost in the minds of the guests; who, on the whole, were just as well pleased to have it so. But, just as before, it was Aunt Bachissia, this time without intending to, who introduced the unwelcome topic. She asked if the report that the “Doctor” was to marry his niece were true or no.

===Chinese===
谈话又继续了一阵，谈的都是些不关痛痒的话题。波蕾达婶婶不时起身出出进进，谈话间或冷了场，随之而来的是几乎叫人发窘的长久的沉默。同*上一次*一样，人人都本能地避开客人们心里最放不下的那件事；客人们呢，总的来说也乐得这样。可是，正像从前那样，又是巴奇西娅婶婶——这一回是出于无心——把这个不受欢迎的话题引了出来。她问，外传“大夫”要娶外甥女的话，是真是假。

===Original===
The Porrus looked at one another, and Grazia, bending her head still lower over her plate, laughed softly to herself.

===Chinese===
波鲁家的人面面相觑，格拉齐娅把头伏得更低地凑在盘子上，暗自轻轻笑了。

===Original===
Paolo glanced at the girl, and, with an irony that seemed a little forced, replied:

===Chinese===
保罗瞟了姑娘一眼，带着一点似乎勉强的讥诮口气回答：

===Original===
“Eh, no! She is going to marry the Very Right Honourable Sub-Prefect!”

===Chinese===
“嗐，不！她要嫁的是那位最尊最贵的副省长大人！”

===Original===
Grazia raised her head with a sudden movement and opened her lips, then as quickly lowered it, the blood meanwhile rushing up to her forehead.

===Chinese===
格拉齐娅霍地抬起头，张开了嘴唇，随即又同样飞快地低下去，血这时涌上了她的额头。

===Original===
“Oh! he’s old,” said Minnia. “I know him; he’s always walking about the station. Ugh! he has a long, red beard, and a high hat.”

===Chinese===
“哦！他老了，”米妮娅说，“我认识他；他老在车站那儿溜达。呸！他留着长长的红胡子，还戴一顶大礼帽。”

===Original===
“A high hat too?”

===Chinese===
“还戴大礼帽？”

===Original===
“Yes, a high hat﻿—a widower.”

===Chinese===
“对，大礼帽——是个鳏夫。”

===Original===
“The high hat is a widower?”

===Chinese===
“大礼帽是鳏夫？”

===Original===
“You shut up!” said the child sharply, turning on her sister.

===Chinese===
“你闭嘴！”孩子厉声说，掉头冲着她的姐姐。

===Original===
“No, I’m not going to shut up. He’s a Freemason; he won’t have his children baptised, or be married in church. That’s the way of it; he’ll not marry in church.”

===Chinese===
“不，我就不闭嘴。他是个共济会会员；他不肯让孩子受洗，也不肯进教堂成婚。就是这么回事；他不会进教堂成婚的。”

===Original===
“The young lady is well informed,” said Uncle Efes Maria, polished as usual.

===Chinese===
“这位小姐真是消息灵通。”埃费斯·马里亚大叔说，一如既往地文雅得体。

===Original===
Thereupon Aunt Porredda, who had almost shrieked aloud at the word “Freemason,” waved both arms in the air, and burst out:

===Chinese===
一听“共济会会员”这个词险些失声叫出来的波蕾达婶婶，顿时双臂向空中乱挥，迸出这么一通话：
===Original===
“Yes, a Freemason! One of those people who pray to the devil. Upon my word, I believe my granddaughter there would just as leave have him! We are all on the road to perdition here, and why not? There’s Grazia, forever reading bad books, and those infernal papers, till now she doesn’t want to go to confession any more! Ah, those prohibited books! I lie awake all night thinking of them. But now, this is what I want to say: Grazia reads bad books; Paolo﻿—you see him, that one over there, Doctor Pededdu﻿—well, he studied on the Continent where they don’t believe in God any more; now that’s all right, at least, it isn’t, it’s all wrong, but you can understand a little why those two poor creatures have stopped believing in God. But the rest of us, who don’t know anything about books and who have never in our lives ridden on a railroad﻿—that devil’s horse﻿—why should *we* cease to believe in God, in our kind Saviour, who died for us on the cross? Why? why? tell me why. You there, Giovanna Era, tell me why you should be willing to marry a man by civil ceremony when you already have a husband living?”

===Chinese===
“对，共济会会员！就是那号向魔鬼祷告的人。不骗你们，我看我那孙女没准儿也乐意嫁他呢！我们这地方人人都在往沉沦的路上走，这又有什么稀奇？就说格拉齐娅吧，坏书一本接一本读，还有那些邪门的报纸，读到如今连忏悔都不肯去了！啊，那些禁书！我整夜整夜睡不着，惦记着它们。可是听着，我要说的是这个：格拉齐娅读坏书；保罗——你瞧见他了吧，就是那边那一位，佩代杜大夫——嗐，他是在大陆上念的书，那边的人已经不信上帝了；唔，这倒也罢了——不对，也不是罢了，压根儿全不对——可你们多少能明白一点，为什么那两个可怜虫会不再信上帝。可是我们其余这些人，跟书本全不沾边，一辈子没坐过一趟火车——那匹魔鬼的马——为什么连*我们*也不信上帝了，不信我们那位仁慈的救主、为我们钉死在十字架上的救主了？为什么？为什么？倒说说为什么。你，乔凡娜·埃拉，你说说，你分明有个活着的丈夫，怎么就肯行世俗婚礼再嫁人？”

===Original===
The final clause of Aunt Porredda’s oration fell with startling effect upon her audience. Grazia, who, with a smile upon her lips, had been busily engaged in rolling pieces of bread into little pellets, raised her head quickly, and the smile died away; Paolo, who, likewise smiling, had been fitting the blade of a knife in and out of the prongs of his fork, straightened himself with a brusque movement; and Uncle Efes Maria turned his dull, round face towards Giovanna, and fixed her with an impassive stare.

===Chinese===
波蕾达婶婶这篇宏论的最后一句，落在满座听众身上，效果惊人。唇边带笑、正忙着把面包搓成小丸子的格拉齐娅，霍地抬起头来，笑意敛去；同样带笑、正把刀刃在叉齿间插进插出的保罗，猛然坐直了身子；埃费斯·马里亚大叔则把他那张迟钝的圆脸转向乔凡娜，无动于衷地盯住了她。

===Original===
Giovanna herself, the object of this wholly unlooked-for attack, though she flushed crimson, replied with cynical indifference:

===Chinese===
乔凡娜本人——这场全然出其不意的攻击的靶子——虽然涨红了脸，答话时却带着冷冷的、满不在乎的神气：

===Original===
“I haven’t any husband, my dear Aunt Porredda. Ask your son over there.”

===Chinese===
“我没有什么丈夫，亲爱的波蕾达婶婶。去问问您那边那位儿子吧。”

===Original===
“My son!” exclaimed the other angrily. “I have no son. He’s a child of the devil!”

===Chinese===
“我的儿子！”对方怒气冲冲地嚷道，“我没有儿子。他是魔鬼的孩子！”

===Original===
It almost seemed as though Giovanna had succeeded in throwing the responsibility of her act upon Paolo, because he had won her case for her!

===Chinese===
乔凡娜这样说，倒像是把干下这桩事的责任推到了保罗头上，因为替她打赢官司的正是他！

===Original===
Every one laughed at Aunt Porredda’s outbreak, even Minnia, and the servant who entered the room at that moment, carrying the cheese. Notwithstanding her wrath, Aunt Porredda took the dish and handed it politely to her guests.

===Chinese===
波蕾达婶婶这一通发作，人人都笑了，连米妮娅也笑了，还有正好这时端着干酪进屋来的女仆。波蕾达婶婶尽管怒气未消，还是接过盘子，客客气气地端给客人们。

===Original===
“Upon my soul,” said Aunt Bachissia, carefully cutting herself a slice, and speaking in a tone of gentle melancholy, “you are as good as gold, there is no doubt about that, but﻿—you live at your ease, you have a house like a church, and a husband like a strong tower (Uncle Efes Maria coughed), and you have a circle of stars about you﻿—motioning towards them﻿—so it is easy enough to talk like that. Ah! if you knew once what it meant to be in want, and to look forward to having to beg your bread in your old age! Do you understand? In your old age!”

===Chinese===
“我拿性命起誓，”巴奇西娅婶婶仔细给自己切下一块，语气里带着一种温柔的忧郁，“您心眼儿好得像金子，这没什么可说的，可是——您过日子安安逸逸，房子像座教堂，丈夫像座坚固的塔（埃费斯·马里亚大叔咳嗽了一声），身边还有一圈星星围着——她朝他们那边比了比——这样的话，说说自然容易。啊！您要是知道一回穷是什么滋味，知道眼巴巴盼着老了要伸手讨饭是什么滋味，就好了！您明白吗？到老了的时候！”

===Original===
“Bravo!” cried Paolo. “But I would like to have a clean knife.”

===Chinese===
“好极了！”保罗喊道，“不过我想要一把干净刀子。”

===Original===
“What difference does that make, Bachissia Era?” answered Aunt Porredda. “You are afraid to trust in Divine Providence, and that means that you have lost your faith in God! How do you know whether you will be poor or rich when you are old? Is not Costantino Ledda coming back some day?”

===Chinese===
“这有什么要紧，巴奇西娅·埃拉？”波蕾达婶婶答道，“你是不敢信靠上天的眷顾，这就是说你把对上帝的信仰丢了！你老了以后是穷是富，你怎么知道？科斯坦蒂诺·莱达不是总有一天要回来吗？”

===Original===
“Yes, to be a beggar too,” said Aunt Bachissia coldly.

===Chinese===
“是啊，回来也照样当叫花子。”巴奇西娅婶婶冷冷地说。

===Original===
“And God alone knows whether he ever will come back,” observed the young lawyer brutally, taking the knife which the servant held out to him, blade foremost.

===Chinese===
“而且他到底回不回得来，只有上帝知道。”年轻的律师不留情面地说了一句，接过女仆递给他的刀子，刀刃冲前。

===Original===
They had all heard that Costantino was ill, and there was a report that his lungs were affected.

===Chinese===
满座的人都已听说科斯坦蒂诺病了，还风传他的肺出了毛病。

===Original===
In order to appear agitated﻿—and possibly she really was so to some extent﻿—Giovanna now hid her face in her hands and said brokenly:

===Chinese===
为了显得心里发慌——说不定她也真有几分发慌——乔凡娜这时把脸埋进双手里，断断续续地说：

===Original===
“Besides﻿—if it is only to be a civil ceremony﻿—it is﻿—it is because﻿—” Then she stopped.

===Chinese===
“再说——要是只行世俗婚礼——那是——那是因为——”说到这里她停住了。
===Original===
“Well, why don’t you go on?” cried Paolo. “You are to be married by civil ceremony because the priests won’t give you any other! They don’t understand, and they never will understand; just as you will never understand, Mamma Porredda. What is marriage, after all? It is a contract made between men, and binding only in the sight of men. The religious ceremony really means nothing at all﻿—”

===Chinese===
“嗐，你怎么不往下说了？”保罗喊道，“你要行世俗婚礼，是因为神父们不肯给你别的婚礼！他们不懂，永远也不会懂；正像您也永远不懂，波蕾达妈妈。婚姻究竟是什么？不过是人与人之间订立的契约，只在人眼前有约束力罢了。宗教婚礼其实一点意义也没有——”

===Original===
“It is a sacrament!” cried Aunt Porredda, beside herself.

===Chinese===
“那是圣事！”波蕾达婶婶按捺不住地喊道。

===Original===
“Means nothing at all,” continued Paolo. “Just as some day the civil ceremony will mean nothing at all. Men and women should be at liberty to enter spontaneously into unions with one another and to dissolve them when they cease to be in harmony. The man﻿—”

===Chinese===
“一点意义也没有，”保罗接着说，“正如将来的某一天，世俗婚礼也会变得一点意义都没有。男人和女人应当有自由，自愿地彼此结合，一旦不再和睦，也应当有自由解除。男人——”

===Original===
“Ah, you are no better than a beast!” exclaimed Aunt Porredda, though it was, in fact, not the first time that she had heard her son express these views. “It is the end of the world. God has grown weary; and who can wonder? He is punishing us; this is the deluge. I have heard that there have been terrible earthquakes already!”

===Chinese===
“啊，你简直连畜生都不如！”波蕾达婶婶嚷道，尽管事实上这并不是她头一回听儿子发表这些见解。“这是世界末日到了。上帝厌倦了；这有什么好奇怪的？他在惩罚我们；这就是那场大洪水。我听说，可怕的地震已经闹过了！”

===Original===
“There have always been earthquakes,” observed Uncle Efes Maria, who did not know whether to side with his wife or his son. Probably, in the bottom of his heart his sympathies were with the former, but he did not want to say so openly for fear of being looked down upon by the gifted Paolo.

===Chinese===
“地震从来就有。”埃费斯·马里亚大叔说；他不知道该站在妻子一边，还是站在儿子一边。多半在他心底里，他是向着前者的，可他不愿明说，生怕被天才的保罗看轻。

===Original===
The latter made no reply. Already he regretted having said so much, being too truly attached to his mother to wish to give her needless pain. Giovanna now took her hands from her face, and spoke in a tone of gentle humility:

===Chinese===
保罗没有答话。他已经后悔说了这么多——他实在是太依恋母亲，不愿给她平白添痛。乔凡娜这才把双手从脸上拿开，用一种温顺谦卑的声调说：

===Original===
“Listen,” said she. “When I was married before﻿—*to that unfortunate*﻿—I had only the civil ceremony, and if he had not been arrested, who knows when we ever would have had the religious marriage! And yet, were we not just as much man and wife? No one ever said a word, and God, who knows all, was not offended﻿—”

===Chinese===
“听着，”她说，“我从前结婚的时候——*跟那个不幸的人*——行的也只是世俗婚礼；要是他没有被捕，谁知道我们哪年哪月才能补行宗教婚礼！可是，我们不照样是两口子吗？谁也没说过一个字，无所不知的上帝也不曾见怪——”

===Original===
“But he punished you,” said Aunt Porredda quickly.

===Chinese===
“可他惩罚了你。”波蕾达婶婶立刻说。

===Original===
“That remains to be seen!” shouted Aunt Bachissia, whose bile was beginning to rise. “Was the punishment for that, or for Basile Ledda’s murder?”

===Chinese===
“那还说不定呢！”巴奇西娅婶婶嚷了起来，她的肝火开始往上冒。“那惩罚是为了这个，还是为了巴西莱·莱达被杀那桩案子？”

===Original===
“If it had been for the murder, only Costantino would have been punished.”

===Chinese===
“要是为了那桩凶案，受罚的就该只有科斯坦蒂诺一个。”

===Original===
“Well,” said the old witch, her green eyes glittering with triumph, “is not that just what I am saying? My Giovanna here is not to be punished any longer for his fault, since God has given her the opportunity to marry a young man who is fond of her, and who will make her forget all her sufferings!”

===Chinese===
“这不正是我说的话吗？”老巫婆说，绿眼睛里闪着得胜的光，“我这乔凡娜不该再替他的罪过受罚了，既然上帝给了她这个机会，好嫁一个疼她的年轻人，一个能叫她忘掉所有苦难的人！”

===Original===
“And who is also rich,” remarked Uncle Efes Maria, and no one could tell whether he spoke ingenuously or no.

===Chinese===
“而且人家还有钱。”埃费斯·马里亚大叔说；谁也说不准他这话是有口无心，还是别有用意。

===Original===
Giovanna, who had quite lost the thread of her discourse, was, nevertheless, determined to continue her role of patient martyr. “Ah, my dear Aunt Porredda,” said she, “you don’t know all, but God, who alone can see into our hearts, he will forgive me even if I live in mortal sin, because he will know that the fault is not with me. I would gladly have the religious ceremony, but it cannot be.”

===Chinese===
乔凡娜早已把自己话头的线索丢了个干净，却仍旧打定主意，要把坚忍殉道者的角色演下去。“啊，亲爱的波蕾达婶婶，”她说，“您并不知道全部底细；可上帝，唯独他能看透我们的心，哪怕我活在死罪里，他也会宽恕我，因为他会知道，过错不在我。我是巴不得行宗教婚礼的，可就是行不成。”

===Original===
“Yes, because you are married already to some one else, you child of the devil!”

===Chinese===
“是啊，因为你早就嫁了别人，你这魔鬼的孩子！”

===Original===
“But that other one is as good as dead! Just tell me now, can he help me to earn a living? And if the lawyers, who are educated and learned, and who know what life really is, can dissolve civil marriages, why can’t the priests dissolve religious ones? Perhaps they don’t understand about it. There is that priest whom we have﻿—Elias Portolu﻿—the one who is so good, you know him? he talks like a saint, and never gets angry with anyone. Well, even he can’t say anything but ‘No, no, no; marriage can only be dissolved by death﻿—and go and be blessed, if you don’t know what is right!’ Does a body have to live? Yes, or no? And when you can’t live, when you are as poor as Job, and can’t get work, and have nothing, nothing, nothing! And just tell me, you, Aunt Porredda, suppose I had been some other woman, and suppose there had been no divorce, what would have happened? Why, mortal sin, that is what would have happened, mortal sin!”

===Chinese===
“可那一个，跟死了也差不离！你倒说说看，他能帮我挣出口饭来吗？要说律师——他们有学问、有见识，知道生活到底是怎么回事——既然他们能解除世俗婚姻，神父们为什么就不能解除宗教婚姻？兴许他们不明白这里头的道理。就说我们那位神父吧——埃利亚斯·波尔托卢（Elias Portolu）——就是那位顶好的神父，你认识他吧？他说起话来像位圣徒，从来不同任何人动气。可是连他也只会说：‘不，不，不；婚姻只有死才能解除——你要是不知道什么叫正道，就去求天主降福吧！’人总得活下去吧？是，还是不是？要是活不下去，当你穷得像约伯，找不着活计，什么都没有，什么都没有、都没有！你再告诉我，你，波蕾达婶婶，假如我是另一个女人，假如没有离婚这回事，那会怎么样？嗐，落个死罪，落个的就是死罪，死罪！”
===Original===
“And in your old age﻿—want,” said Aunt Bachissia.

===Chinese===
“还有你老了的时候——受穷。”巴奇西娅婶婶说。

===Original===
The servant brought in the fruit: bunches of black, shining, dried grapes, and wrinkled pears, as yellow as autumn leaves.

===Chinese===
女仆端进来水果：一串串黑得发亮的干葡萄，还有皱皮的梨，黄得像秋叶一样。

===Original===
The old hostess handed the dish to her old guest, with an indescribable look of compassion. Her anger, and disdain, and indignation had suddenly melted away as she realised the sordid natures of the mother and daughter. “Good San Francisco, forgive them,” she prayed inwardly. “Because they are so ignorant, and blind, and hard!” Then she said mildly: “You and I, Bachissia Era, are old women, and you, Giovanna, will be old some day. Now tell me one thing: what is it that comes after old age?”

===Chinese===
年老的女主人把果盘递给年老的女客，脸上带着一种难以形容的怜悯。她的怒气、轻蔑和义愤忽然间消融了，因为她看清了这母女俩卑污的心性。“仁慈的圣方济各啊，饶恕她们吧，”她在心里祷告，“因为她们是这样无知、盲目、心硬！”随后她温和地说：“您和我，巴奇西娅·埃拉，都是老太婆了；而你，乔凡娜，也总有一天会老。现在告诉我一件事：老了之后，接着来的是什么？”

===Original===
“Why, death.”

===Chinese===
“嗐，死呗。”

===Original===
“Death; yes, death comes after. And after death what is there?”

===Chinese===
“死；对，老了就是死。死了之后呢，还有什么？”

===Original===
“Eternity?” said Paolo, laughing softly to himself as he devoured his grapes like a greedy child, holding the bunch close to his mouth, and detaching the seeds with his sharp little teeth.

===Chinese===
“永恒？”保罗轻声笑着问，一面像贪嘴的孩子那样吃着他的葡萄——把葡萄串凑到嘴边，用他那口尖细的小牙把一粒粒葡萄剔下来。

===Original===
“Eternity, precisely; eternity comes after﻿—where are you going, Minnia? Stay where you are.” But the child, tired of the conversation, slipped out of the room. “What do *you* say, Giovanna Era, does eternity follow? yes, or no? Bachissia Era﻿—yes, or no?”

===Chinese===
“正是永恒；死后来的就是永恒——你上哪儿去，米妮娅？待在原地别动。”可孩子听腻了这场谈话，溜出了屋子。“*你*说呢，乔凡娜·埃拉，到底有没有永恒？有，还是没有？巴奇西娅·埃拉——有，还是没有？”

===Original===
“Yes,” said the guests.

===Chinese===
“有。”客人们说。

===Original===
“Yes? and yet you never think of it?”

===Chinese===
“有？可你们从来不想着它？”

===Original===
“Oh! what is the use of thinking of it?” said Paolo, getting up, and wiping his mouth with his napkin; he felt that it was high time for him to be off; he had already wasted too much time on these women, who, after all, were interesting solely from the fact that they had not yet paid him. “There are some people waiting to see me at the office﻿—several people, in fact,” he said. “I will see you again; you are not leaving yet awhile?”

===Chinese===
“嗐！想它有什么用？”保罗说着站起身来，用餐巾擦了擦嘴；他觉得动身的时辰早到了；他在这些女人身上已经耽搁了太多工夫——她们之所以还值得一提，说到底，无非是因为她们还没有付他钱。“事务所里有人等着见我——其实，好几位呢。”他说，“回头再见；你们一时半会儿还不走吧？”

===Original===
“Tomorrow morning at daybreak.”

===Chinese===
“明天一早，天一亮就走。”

===Original===
“Not really? Oh! you had better stay longer,” he said indifferently, as he struggled into his huge overcoat. When it was on, Aunt Bachissia﻿—watching him out of her sharp green eyes﻿—thought that the little Doctor looked like a *magia*, that is, one of those grotesque and frightening figures whom wizards evoke by their arts.

===Chinese===
“当真不留下？哦！你们还是多住些日子好。”他漠然说着，一面往那件肥大的外套里挣着穿。外套上了身，巴奇西娅婶婶——拿她那双尖利的绿眼睛瞧着他——觉得这位小小的“大夫”活像一个 *magia*，也就是巫师作法召出来的那号怪诞骇人的东西。

===Original===
He departed, and immediately afterwards Miss Grazia, who had hardly spoken throughout the entire meal, arose and left the room as well. Uncle Efes Maria settled himself back in his chair, and began to read the *New Sardinia*. Bursts of laughter came from the two girls in the kitchen, and the women sat, each eating a pear, in perfect silence. A weight hung over them; upon Aunt Porredda as well as upon the others, for she was realising in her simple untutored mind that the disease that had attacked the souls of her ignorant guests was one and the same as that from which her sophisticated son and granddaughter were suffering.

===Chinese===
他走了；紧接着，整顿饭几乎一句话没说的格拉齐娅小姐也起身离了席。埃费斯·马里亚大叔往椅背上一靠，读起《*新撒丁报*》来。厨房里传来那两个姑娘的一阵阵大笑；女人们坐着，一人吃着一只梨，全然无声。一片沉重压在她们每个人的心头；也压在波蕾达婶婶心上，同别人一样——因为她正用她那颗淳朴未凿的头脑渐渐明白：侵染她那些无知客人灵魂的病，同她那精于世故的儿子和孙女所患的，原是同一种病。

## Part I / 第一部

## Part II / 第二部

## V / 第五章

===Original===
Sunday morning, a fortnight later, found all the personages of our story assembled at Mass, with Priest Elias officiating. The country people said that when he celebrated he seemed to have wings.

===Chinese===
两星期后的一个星期日早晨，我们这个故事里的全部人物都聚在一起望弥撒，由埃利亚斯神父主礼。乡下人都说，他主持弥撒的时候，仿佛身上长出了翅膀。

===Original===
Giovanna alone was absent; and this for two reasons. First, her late misfortune required the observance of a sort of mourning; she was expected not to show herself outside the house except when her work made it necessary. Apart from this, however, she had fallen into a state of lethargy, and appeared to be quite unable to move about, to go anywhere, to work, or even to pray. She had, indeed, never been much of a Christian at any time, though before the trial she had made a vow to walk barefoot to a certain church in the mountains, and, if Costantino were acquitted, to drag herself on her hands and knees from the point where the church first came into view to its doors; that is, a distance of about two kilometres.

===Chinese===
只有乔凡娜一个人缺席；这有两层缘故。第一，她新近遭的不幸，需要遵行一种近乎居丧的规矩；除了活计上的必需，人们不指望她在屋子外面抛头露面。但撇开这层不谈，她还陷入了一种昏沉委顿的状态，仿佛全然动弹不得，去不了任何地方，做不动活计，甚至连祷告也做不来了。说实话，无论什么时候，她本来就算不上一个多虔诚的基督徒，尽管开庭之前她曾许下心愿，要赤着脚走到山里的一座教堂去，倘若科斯坦蒂诺获判无罪，她便从第一眼望得见那座教堂的地方起，用手和膝盖拖着自己，一路爬到教堂门口；也就是说，爬上大约两公里的路程。

===Original===
Now, she had ceased praying, or talking, or eating, and even seemed to have lost all interest in her child. Aunt Bachissia had to feed him with bread crumbled up in milk in order to keep the poor little fellow alive. Some of the neighbours said that Giovanna was losing her mind; and indeed it did look so. She would remain for hours at a time in a sort of stupor, crouched in a corner with her glassy eyes fixed on vacancy, and when she aroused it was only to fly into violent paroxysms, tearing her hair, and crying out wildly.

===Chinese===
如今，她不再祷告，不再说话，不再吃东西，甚至连孩子也好像完全不上她的心了。巴奇西娅婶婶只得把面包掰碎了泡在牛奶里喂他，好歹保住这可怜的小家伙一条命。有些邻居说，乔凡娜快要疯了；看那样子也确实像。她会一连好几个钟头蹲在一个角落里，处于一种恍惚麻木的状态，一双呆滞的眼睛直勾勾地盯着虚空；而她一旦惊醒过来，也无非是猛烈地发作一场，撕扯自己的头发，狂呼乱叫。

===Original===
After the final interview with Costantino, when she had had the child with her, she could think of nothing else, and described the scene in the prison over and over again, with the monotonous insistence of a monomaniac:

===Chinese===
自从同科斯坦蒂诺最后会过那一面之后——那次她是带着孩子去的——她就再想不了别的事，一遍又一遍地讲说狱中的那番情景，带着偏执狂那种单调的执拗：

===Original===
“He was there, and he was laughing. He was livid, and yet he laughed, standing there behind the bars. Malthineddu seized hold of the bars, and he touched his little hands and then he laughed! My heart! my heart! don’t laugh like that; it hurts me, because I know that that is how dead people laugh! And the guards, standing there like harpies! At first they were good to us, those guards who watch over human flesh; but afterwards, when Costantino had been condemned, they were cruel, as cruel as dogs! Malthinu was frightened when he saw them, and cried; and his father laughed! Do you understand? The baby, the little, innocent thing, cried; he understood that his father had been condemned, and he cried! Oh, my heart! my heart!”

===Chinese===
“他在那儿，他在笑。他脸色铁青，可他还在笑，站在铁栏杆后头。马尔蒂内杜抓住栏杆，他碰了碰那双小手，然后就笑了！我的心啊！我的心啊！别那样笑；那样笑我心疼，因为我知道，死人就是那样笑的！还有那些看守，站在那儿活像一群妖鸟！起初他们待我们还好，那些看守活人肉身的看守；可是后来，科斯坦蒂诺定了罪，他们就狠毒起来，狠毒得像恶狗！马尔蒂努一见他们就吓哭了；可他的父亲还在笑！你明白吗？那娃娃，那个小小的、无辜的小东西，哭了；他明白他的父亲被定了罪，他就哭了！啊，我的心啊！我的心啊！”

===Original===
Then Aunt Bachissia, beside herself with impatience, and unable to hold in any longer, would exclaim:

===Chinese===
于是巴奇西娅婶婶就会急得没了耐性，再也按捺不住，嚷道：

===Original===
“Honestly, Giovanna, anyone would take you to be two years old! That child there has more sense than you. Simpleton!” And sometimes she would threaten to beat her; but prayers, sympathy, and threats were equally unavailing.

===Chinese===
“说实在的，乔凡娜，人家准当你是两岁的娃娃！那边那个孩子都比你有见识。傻瓜！”有时她还吓唬着要揍她；可是祷告也好，怜惜也好，威吓也好，一概无济于事。

===Original===
Meanwhile, word came from Nuoro that, while waiting to hear from the appeal, Costantino had been removed to the jurisdiction of Cagliari. Then came a short, sad, little letter from the prisoner himself. The journey had gone well, but there, at Cagliari, the heat was suffocating, and certain red insects, and others of different colours, tormented him night and day. He sent a kiss to the child, and urged Giovanna to bring him up in the fear of God. He also asked to be remembered to his friend Isidoro. On this Sunday, therefore, at the close of the Mass, Aunt Bachissia waited till the fisherman should have finished singing the sacred lauds in his ringing voice, in order to deliver Costantino’s message.

===Chinese===
这时节，努奥罗那边传来消息说，科斯坦蒂诺在等候上诉结果期间，已被移解到卡利亚里（Cagliari）的司法辖区。随后囚徒本人来了一封短短的、愁惨的小信。一路走得顺当，只是卡利亚里那边热得叫人窒息，还有些红色的虫子，另有别种颜色的，日夜折磨着他。他给孩子送上一个吻，叮嘱乔凡娜要在敬畏上帝之中把孩子抚养成人。他还托着向他的朋友伊西多罗问好。因此，这个星期日，弥撒结束的时候，巴奇西娅婶婶等着那渔人用他洪亮的嗓音唱完圣赞歌，好传达科斯坦蒂诺的口信。

===Original===
Priest Elias remained kneeling on the steps of the high altar, with white ecstatic face, and Isidoro still sang on, but the people began to leave, filing past Aunt Bachissia, as she stood waiting.

===Chinese===
埃利亚斯神父还跪在主祭坛的台阶上，一张出神的脸白得发亮；伊西多罗还在唱下去，可人们已经开始散去，一个接一个从伫立等候的巴奇西娅婶婶身边鱼贯而过。

===Original===
Aunt Martina passed, with the fiery bearing of a blooded steed, old but indomitable still; Brontu passed, dressed in a new suit of clothes, his hair shining with oil; he railed at the priests, but on Sunday he went to Mass; and Giacobbe passed, in a pair of new linen trousers, smelling strong of the shop. Still Isidoro sang on.

===Chinese===
马丁娜婶婶走过去了，带着一匹纯种骏马般的火爆做派，人老了，那股不服输的劲头却还在；布龙图走过去了，穿着一身新衣裳，头发抹得油光锃亮；他骂起教士来不留情面，可一到星期日照样来望弥撒；贾科贝也走过去了，穿着一条新亚麻布裤子，一股子铺子里浓重的布料味。伊西多罗还在唱。

===Original===
The church, at last, became almost empty; the fisherman’s sonorous voice resounded among the dusty, white rafters; the boards and beams of the roof; the side altars, covered with coarse cloths, adorned with paper flowers, and presided over by melancholy saints of painted wood.

===Chinese===
教堂里终于差不多空了；渔人那洪亮的嗓音回荡在积尘的白色椽木之间，回荡在屋顶的木板与梁木之间，回荡在那些侧坛之间——侧坛罩着粗布，缀着纸花，由一尊尊忧郁的彩绘木圣像坐镇着。

===Original===
When Uncle Isidoro stopped at length, there were only the priest, a boy who was extinguishing the candles, Aunt Bachissia, and an old blind man left.

===Chinese===
等到伊西多罗大叔终于停下来时，剩下的只有神父、一个正吹熄蜡烛的男孩、巴奇西娅婶婶，和一个瞎眼的老人。

===Original===
Isidoro had to repeat the final response to the lauds himself; then he got up, put away the little bell used to mark the Stations of the Rosary, and moved towards Aunt Bachissia, who stood waiting for him near the door. They went out together, and she gave him Costantino’s message; then she begged him to do her a favour; it was to ask Priest Elias to go to see Giovanna and try to reason her out of the condition she had allowed herself to fall into. He promised to do so, and they separated.

===Chinese===
圣赞歌最后的一句应答，只得由伊西多罗自己一个人念完；随后他站起身，收起那把用来点明玫瑰经各端的小铃，朝站在门口等他的巴奇西娅婶婶走过去。两人一同出了教堂，她把科斯坦蒂诺的口信告诉了他；接着又求他帮个忙，就是去请埃利亚斯神父来看看乔凡娜，劝一劝她，让她从任由自己沉陷下去的境地中回过神来。他答应照办，两人便分了手。

===Original===
On the way home Aunt Bachissia was joined by Giacobbe Dejas, who had been standing on the open square before the church, looking down at the village and the yellow fields, all bathed in sunlight.

===Chinese===
回家的路上，贾科贝·德亚斯同巴奇西娅婶婶走到了一处；他方才一直站在教堂前的空场上，俯望着村子和大片大片沐浴在阳光里的黄色田野。
===Original===
“How are you?” asked the herdsman.

===Chinese===
“你好啊？”牧人问道。

===Original===
“Ah, good Lord! bad enough, without being actually ill. And you, how do you like your new place?”

===Chinese===
“唉，老天爷！说病倒不是病，反正够受的。你呢，新东家那儿合意吗？”

===Original===
“Oh! I told you how it would be. I’m out of the frying-pan into the fire! The old woman is as close as the devil; she expects me to work till I fall to pieces, and will hardly let me come in to Mass once a fortnight.”

===Chinese===
“嗐！我早跟你说过会怎么样的。我是才出煎锅，又入了火坑！那老太婆抠门抠得赛过魔鬼；她要我干活干到散架为止，连半个月来望一回弥撒都难得放我。”

===Original===
“And the master?”

===Chinese===
“那么男主人呢？”

===Original===
“Oh! the master? Well, he’s just a little beast, that’s all.”

===Chinese===
“嗐！男主人？哼，他就是个小畜生，就这么回事。”

===Original===
“What do you mean by saying such a thing as that, Giacobbe?”

===Chinese===
“你怎么说出这种话来，贾科贝？”

===Original===
“Well, it’s the simple truth, little spring bird. He growls and snarls over every trifle, and gets drunk, and lies like time. I suppose Isidoro Pane told you﻿—” He paused, and Aunt Bachissia, fixing her small green eyes upon him, reflected that, if he talked like that about his master, he must have some object.

===Chinese===
“咳，这可是不掺假的实话，小春鸟。他为一点鸡毛蒜皮的小事就咆哮就狂吠，喝得醉醺醺，撒起谎来没个边儿。我估摸着伊西多罗·帕内都跟你说过了——”他顿住了。巴奇西娅婶婶把她那双小小的绿眼睛盯在他身上，心里琢磨，他要是这样讲他的东家，背后必定有什么名堂。

===Original===
“Well,” he resumed, “Isidoro Pane must have told you﻿—of course he told you, about Brontu being drunk that evening; it was just here, where we are now, Brontu yelled out: ‘Tell Giovanna Era that if she gets a divorce I’ll marry her!’ The beast, that’s just what he is, a beast! He drinks brandy by the cask.”

===Chinese===
“是这么回事，”他又接上话头，“伊西多罗·帕内一定跟你说了——他当然说了，说布龙图那天晚上喝醉的事；就在这儿，就是咱们眼下站的这个地方，布龙图嚷出来的：‘告诉乔凡娜·埃拉，她要是离了婚，我就娶她！’畜生，他天生就是个畜生！他喝白兰地是论桶喝的。”

===Original===
Of the last clause of this speech, however, Aunt Bachissia took in not one word. The fact that Brontu had said he would marry Giovanna if she got a divorce was all she comprehended. Her green eyes flashed as she asked haughtily: “And you wish him not to, Giacobbe?”

===Chinese===
可是这段话里的最后一个分句，巴奇西娅婶婶一个字也没有听进去。布龙图说过，乔凡娜只要离了婚，他就娶她——她领会到的就只有这一桩。她那双绿眼睛里闪出光来，傲然问道：“这么说，你是巴不得他不娶咯，贾科贝？”

===Original===
“I? What difference would it make to me, little spring bird? But you ought to be ashamed of yourself to think of such a thing, Aunt Kite, hardly two weeks after﻿—”

===Chinese===
“我？那与我什么相干，小春鸟？倒是你该自个儿害臊，居然动起这种念头来，鸢婶婶，前后还不到两个星期——”

===Original===
“I’m not a kite,” snapped the old woman angrily; and though the other laughed, she could see that he too was furious.

===Chinese===
“我才不是什么鸢，”老太太怒冲冲地顶了一句；对方虽然哈哈笑着，她却看得出，他心里也憋着一股火。

===Original===
“You might, at least, wait to hear from the appeal,” said he. “And then you can devour Costantino as you would a lamb without spot. Yes, devour him if you want to, but I can tell you that Giovanna will get a brandy-bottle for a husband, and just as long as Martina Dejas is alive you will starve worse than ever.”

===Chinese===
“你们至少也该等等上诉的消息再讲，”他说。“到那时，你们再把科斯坦蒂诺当一只无瑕的羔羊那样吞吃不迟。是啊，想吞就吞吧；可我告诉你，乔凡娜到手的丈夫会是一只白兰地酒瓶，而且只要马丁娜·德亚斯多活一天，你们就要挨饿，饿得比从前任何时候都厉害。”

===Original===
“Ah! you bald-pate﻿—” began Aunt Bachissia. But Giacobbe walked rapidly away, and she had only the satisfaction of hurling abuse at his retreating back. Not that she proposed to have Giovanna apply for a divorce. Heaven forbid! With poor Costantino still under appeal, and waiting there in that fiery furnace, devoured by horrible insects! No, indeed, but﻿—what right had that vile servant to talk of his master so? What business was it of his to meddle in his master’s concerns? And Aunt Bachissia decided then and there that that “bald raven” had himself taken a fancy to Giovanna; and, filled with this new idea, she reached the cottage.

===Chinese===
“啊！你这秃脑壳——”巴奇西娅婶婶刚开了个头。可是贾科贝快步走开了，她只落得个痛快：朝着他走远的背影一顿臭骂。倒不是她当真打算让乔凡娜去申请离婚。老天爷不容！可怜的科斯坦蒂诺还在上诉，还在那座烈火熊熊的火炉里熬着，被可怕的虫子啃噬着呢！不，自然不；只不过——那个下贱的帮工，有什么权利这样讲他的东家？东家的事，干他什么事，要他来插手？于是巴奇西娅婶婶当场认定，那只“秃乌鸦”准是自己看上了乔凡娜；她满脑子揣着这个新念头，一路回到了农舍。

===Original===
Her immediate thought was to repeat the whole story to Giovanna, but finding her, for the first time in two weeks, bathed, and tranquilly engaged in combing out her long hair, which fell down in heavy, tumbled masses, she was afraid to say a word.

===Chinese===
她的头一个念头，是把这桩事原原本本讲给乔凡娜听；可是她发现女儿两个星期以来头一回洗过了身子，正安安静静地梳理着那头长发——浓密的发缕蓬蓬乱乱地披垂下来——她便一个字也不敢说了。

## VI / 第六章

===Original===
Time passed by; the autumn came, and then the winter. Costantino’s appeal had, of course, been rejected, as appeals always are. One night he was fastened by a chain to another convict, whom he had never seen, and the two took their places in a long file of others, all dressed in linen, all silent; like a drove of wild beasts controlled by some invisible power. They were going﻿—where? They did not know. They were silent﻿—why? They could not say. Presently they were all marched down to the water’s edge, put on board a long, black steamer, and shut into a cage﻿—still like wild beasts. All about them lay the crystal sea, across whose dark, green waters the ruby and emerald reflections from the ship’s lights danced and sparkled like strings of glittering jewels; while above, engirdling the great ring of water, hung the deep blue sky, like an immense, silent vale dotted over with yellow, starry flowers. At first Costantino’s sensations were not altogether unhappy. True, he was going into the unknown to fulfil a cruel destiny, but down in the bottom of his heart he firmly believed that before very long he would be liberated, and he never lost hope.

===Chinese===
时间一天天过去；秋天来了，接着又是冬天。科斯坦蒂诺的上诉当然被驳回了——上诉从来都是这个下场。一天夜里，他被一条铁链和另一个素不相识的囚犯锁在一起，两人排进一长列同样的人中间：个个穿着亚麻布衣，个个默不作声，像一群被某种看不见的力量驾驭的野兽。他们要去哪里？——他们不知道。他们为什么沉默？——他们说不上来。随后，他们全被押到水边，赶上一艘长长的黑色轮船，关进一只笼子里——依旧像一群野兽。四周是水晶般的大海，暗绿色的水面上，船灯红宝石、绿宝石似的反光翩翩起舞、闪闪发亮，像一串串晶莹的珠宝；头顶上，环抱着那一大圈海水，悬着深蓝色的天空，宛如一座巨大而寂静的山谷，缀满黄色的星形花朵。起初，科斯坦蒂诺的心绪并非全然愁苦。不错，他正走向一个未知的地方，去履行一桩残酷的命运；可是在他心底，他坚信过不了多久自己就会获释，他从不曾失去希望。

===Original===
The bustle on deck, the rattle of the chains, and the first motion of the ship as it got under way, filled him with childish curiosity. He had never been to sea, but, as a boy, he had often stood scanning the horizon, and gazing at the grey stretch of the Mediterranean, sometimes dotted over with the white wings of sailing vessels. At such times, as he stood among the wild shrubs and undergrowth of his native mountains, he would dream of some day crossing that far-away sea to distant, unknown lands, and to the golden cities of the Continent. He could read and write, and had a book in which St. Peter’s at Rome was depicted; and in the chapter on sacred history there was an engraving of ancient Jerusalem. Ah! Jerusalem. According to his ideas, Jerusalem must be the finest and largest city in the world; and, as he stood there dreaming among the bushes on Mount Bellu, and gazing off at the grey Mediterranean, it was to Jerusalem that he longed to go. And now, here he was crossing the sea; but how different from his dreams! Yet, so splendid was his conception of Jerusalem that if it had been thither that he was bound, even a chained and condemned prisoner on his way to expiate a crime, he would, nevertheless, have been content to go.

===Chinese===
甲板上的忙乱、铁链的哗啦声，还有轮船启航时第一次的颠动，都让他充满孩子气的好奇。他从来没有出过海；可是少年时代，他常常伫立着眺望远方的地平线，凝视那一带灰蒙蒙的地中海，海面上有时点缀着帆船的白色翅膀。每逢这种时候，他站在家乡山间的野灌木和草丛里，就会梦想有朝一日渡过那片遥远的大海，到遥远的、不知名的土地上去，到大陆上金色的城市里去。他识字，也会写字，有一本书上画着罗马的圣彼得大教堂；在讲圣史的那一章里，还有一幅古耶路撒冷的版画。啊！耶路撒冷。照他的想法，耶路撒冷一定是世界上最壮丽、最大的城市；当他站在贝卢山的灌木丛中做着梦，遥望灰色的地中海时，他一心向往的就是耶路撒冷。如今，他真的在渡海了；可是与当年的梦多么不同啊！不过，他对耶路撒冷的想象是那样辉煌，倘若此行的目的地正是那里，哪怕他是个戴着锁链、要去赎一桩罪的囚犯，他也会心甘情愿地走上这一趟。

===Original===
The pitching and rolling of the ship was accompanied by the ceaseless rush of the water from the bows. Some of the convicts chattered among themselves, laughing and cracking jokes. Costantino fell asleep and dreamed, as he always did, that he was at home again. He had been set free almost immediately﻿—he dreamed﻿—and had gone home without letting Giovanna know a word about it so as to give her the unutterable joy of the surprise. She kept saying: “But this is a dream, this is a dream﻿—” The expenses of the trial had stripped the little house bare of everything, even the bed was gone; but nothing made any difference. All the riches in the world could not compare with the bliss of being free and of living with Giovanna and Malthineddu. But he was terribly tired, so he curled himself up in the baby’s cradle; the cradle rocked, harder and harder all the time. Giovanna laughed and called out: “Be careful not to fall out, Costantino, my dear, my lamb!” And the cradle rocked more than ever. At first he laughed as well, but all at once he found he was suffering, then he fell head foremost on the ground, and woke up.

===Chinese===
船身颠簸摇晃，船头的水声奔流不息。有几个囚犯互相闲谈，笑着逗趣。科斯坦蒂诺睡着了，像往常一样，梦见自己又回到了家。他几乎立刻就被释放了——他梦见——他回到家，事先一个字也没让乔凡娜知道，好让她领受那份难以言喻的惊喜。她不住地说：“这是梦，这是梦——”诉讼的花费把小屋刮了个精光，连床都没了；可这都无关紧要。世上全部的财宝，也比不上自由自在、与乔凡娜和马尔蒂内杜共同生活的幸福。可是他累得要命，便蜷起身子，躺进了婴儿的摇篮；摇篮摇着，越摇越厉害。乔凡娜笑着喊道：“当心别摔出来，科斯坦蒂诺，亲爱的，我的小羊羔！”摇篮摇得愈发厉害了。起初他也笑，可忽然间他觉得自己在受苦，接着便头朝下栽到地上，醒了过来。

===Original===
There was a heavy sea on, and Costantino was sick. The ship struggled up to mountain-heights and then plunged swiftly into bottomless gulfs of water, the waves breaking even over the third deck.

===Chinese===
海上风浪大作，科斯坦蒂诺晕船了。轮船挣扎着爬上山峰般的高处，又猛地扎进深不见底的水渊，浪头甚至一直打到第三层甲板上。

===Original===
All the convicts were ill; some still attempted to joke, while others swore, and one, with a yellow, cunning face﻿—he was Costantino’s companion﻿—moaned and lamented like a child.

===Chinese===
囚犯们全都病了；有的还想说笑，有的破口大骂；有一个生着一张蜡黄的、狡黠的脸——正是锁在科斯坦蒂诺身边的那个——像小孩子一样呻吟哀叹。

===Original===
“Oh!” he groaned, cowering down, gasping and frightened. “I was dreaming that I was at home, and now﻿—now﻿—oh! dear St. Francis, have pity on me!”

===Chinese===
“哦！”他蜷缩着身子呻吟，喘着气，惶惶不安。“我方才还梦见自己在家呢，可如今——如今——哦！好圣方济各（St. Francis），可怜可怜我吧！”

===Original===
Notwithstanding his own misery, both physical and mental, Costantino felt sorry for him. “Patience, my brother, I was dreaming too about being at home.”

===Chinese===
尽管自己身心交困，科斯坦蒂诺还是觉得他可怜。“忍耐些，我的兄弟，我也梦见过回家了。”

===Original===
“I feel,” cried another, “as though my soul were melting away. What the devil is the matter with this ship! It seems to be trying to dance the Sardian dance!” Whereat some of the others still had sufficient spirit left to laugh.

===Chinese===
“我觉得，”另一个嚷道，“我的魂儿都要化掉了。这条船到底见了什么鬼！它怕是想跳撒丁舞吧！”这话引得另外几个人还有精神笑出声来。

===Original===
The storm was increasing. At times Costantino thought he was dying, and was frightened; yet, on the other hand, he felt an unutterable weariness of life. His soul seemed to be steeped in the same bitter fluid that his stomach was casting up. Never, not even at the moment when the sentence of condemnation had been passed upon him, had he experienced anything like his present condition of hopeless misery. He too began to swear and groan, doubling his fists, and twisting his chilled toes. “May you die just as I am dying now, you murderous dogs, who brought all this on me!” he muttered, while tears as bitter as gall welled up into his eyes.

===Chinese===
风暴越来越猛。科斯坦蒂诺时而觉得自己快要死了，心里害怕；可另一方面，他又对生命感到一种难以言传的厌倦。他的灵魂仿佛也浸在他胃里翻上来的那种苦水里。从来不曾有过——连宣判有罪的那一刻也不曾有过——他像现在这样陷于绝望的悲苦。他也开始咒骂、呻吟起来，攥紧双拳，蜷扭着冻僵的脚趾。“愿你们也像我此刻这样死去，你们这些害人的狗东西，是你们把我弄到这步田地的！”他喃喃地骂着，苦胆一般的眼泪涌上了眼眶。

===Original===
Towards dawn the wind subsided, but even when the sickness had passed, Costantino found no relief; he felt as though he had been beaten to the point of death, and he was shaking with cold, and exhaustion, and dread. The steamer relentlessly pursued its way. Oh, if it would only stop for just one moment! A single moment of quiet, it seemed to Costantino, would suffice to restore his strength; but this continuous forging ahead, the constant rolling, the never-ceasing roar of the waves as they lashed the sides of the vessel, kept him in a state of nervous tremor. On, and on, and on; the long hours of agony dragged slowly by; night came again; and all the time his subtle-faced, yellow-visaged companion hardly ceased to sigh and lament, driving Costantino into a perfect frenzy of irritation. Sleep came at length, and then, strange to relate, he had the same dream as on the previous night, only this time it was Giovanna who was in the cradle, and the cradle was rocking quite gently.

===Chinese===
将近黎明，风势平息了；可是即使晕船的滋味过去之后，科斯坦蒂诺也丝毫不见轻松；他觉得自己仿佛被人打了个半死，又是冷，又是疲惫，又是恐惧，浑身直抖。轮船毫不容情地赶着它的路。哦，哪怕只停一停，停片刻也好啊！在科斯坦蒂诺想来，只消片刻的安宁，就足以让他恢复气力；可是这不停的疾驶、不住的摇晃、海浪抽打船舷时永无休止的咆哮，使他一直处在神经质的战栗之中。前行，前行，再前行；漫长的痛楚时光慢吞吞地挨过去；夜又来了；而那位面相狡黠、脸色蜡黄的同伴一路几乎叹息哀号个不停，把科斯坦蒂诺烦得火冒三丈。睡眠终于来了；说来也怪，他又做了前一晚那个同样的梦，只是这一回躺在摇篮里的是乔凡娜，摇篮也摇得十分轻柔。

===Original===
When Costantino awoke, the boat seemed hardly to move; in the silence that precedes the dawn, he heard a voice say: “That is Procida.”

===Chinese===
科斯坦蒂诺醒来时，船仿佛几乎不动了；在黎明前的静寂中，他听见一个声音说：“那是普罗奇达（Procida）。”

===Original===
He was shaking with cold, and wondered if they were to land there, where, he thought he remembered to have heard, the galleys were.

===Chinese===
他冷得发抖，心里犯嘀咕：他们是不是要在此地上岸——他仿佛记得听人说过，苦役营就在这地方。

===Original===
Presently his companion awoke, shivering and yawning prodigiously.

===Chinese===
不久，他的同伴醒了，哆哆嗦嗦，打着老大的哈欠。

===Original===
“Are we there?” asked Costantino. “How do you feel?”

===Chinese===
“到了吗？”科斯坦蒂诺问。“你觉得怎么样？”

===Original===
“Pretty well. Are we there?”

===Chinese===
“还不错。到了吗？”

===Original===
“I don’t know; we are near Procida; is that where the galleys are?”

===Chinese===
“不知道；咱们已经到普罗奇达附近了；苦役营是在那儿吗？”

===Original===
“No; they’re at Nisida,” said the other. “But we are not galley-birds!” he added, with a touch of pride, and then fell to yawning again. “Oh, how I was dreaming!” he said, and then stopped, overcome by the memory of his dream.

===Chinese===
“不；苦役营在尼西达（Nisida），”对方说。“不过咱们又不是服苦役的！”他带着几分得意补充道，随后又打起哈欠来。“哦，我这梦做得好凶！”他说，随即顿住，被梦的回忆压得说不下去了。

===Original===
The prisoners were landed at Naples and immediately placed in a black-and-yellow van, something like a movable sepulchre. Costantino caught a brief glimpse of a wide expanse of smooth green water, a quantity of huge steamers, and innumerable small craft filled with gaily dressed men who shouted out all manner of incomprehensible things. All around the boats, on the surface of the green water, floated weeds, scraps of paper, refuse of all kinds. Enormous buildings were outlined against a sky of deepest blue. At Naples, the convicts were separated; Costantino was taken off to the prison at X﻿—﻿— and saw his yellow-visaged companion no more.

===Chinese===
囚犯们在那不勒斯（Naples）上岸，随即被塞进一辆黑黄两色的囚车——那东西颇像一口会移动的坟墓。科斯坦蒂诺匆匆瞥见一片开阔平滑的绿水、许多巨大的轮船，还有数不清的小船，船上坐满衣着鲜亮的人，嚷着各种听不懂的话。船的四周，绿色的水面上漂着水草、纸片和各色垃圾。一座座庞大的建筑物映在深蓝色的天幕上。在那不勒斯，囚犯们被分开了；科斯坦蒂诺被送往X———的监狱，从此再没见过他那位蜡黄脸的同伴。

===Original===
On reaching his destination, Costantino was at once consigned to a cell where he was to pass the first six months of his term in solitary confinement. This cell measured hardly two metres in length by six palms in breadth: it was furnished with a rude folding bed, which, during the day, was closed and fastened against the wall. From the tiny window nothing could be seen but a strip of sky.

===Chinese===
一到目的地，科斯坦蒂诺立刻被关进一间牢房，要在里面单独监禁，度过刑期的头六个月。这间牢房长不足两米，宽只有六拃：屋里陈设着一张粗糙的折叠床，白天折起来贴墙扣住。从那扇小小的窗口望出去，除了窄窄一条天空，什么也看不见。

===Original===
Of the entire term of his imprisonment this was the dreariest period. He would sit immovable for hours with his legs crossed and his hands clasped about his knee﻿—thinking; but strangely enough he never either lost hope or rebelled against his fate. He was persuaded that what he was enduring was in expiation of that mortal sin, as he regarded it, of having lived with a woman to whom he had not been married by religious ceremony, and he felt an absolute certainty that, this sin atoned for, his innocence would some day be established and he would be set free. At the same time, although he did not despair, he suffered acutely, and passed the days, hours, minutes in a state of nervous expectation of some change that never came, and a prey to a devouring homesickness. Thus day by day, hour by hour, moment by moment, he lived in his thoughts close to Giovanna and the child, recalling with minute precision every little unimportant detail of the cottage life, his past existence, and the happiness that had once been his. In addition, moreover, to his own misery, he suffered at the thought of what Giovanna was enduring: now and again an access of passionate tenderness, having her far more than the child for its object, would seize him and arouse him from his usual state of pensive melancholy; then, leaping to his feet, he would stride back and forth﻿—two, or at most three, steps bringing him to the opposite wall, where he would presently stop, and, throwing himself against it, would beat his head as though trying to dash out his brains. These were his moments of utmost desperation.

===Chinese===
在整个服刑期间，这段日子是最凄凉的。他常常盘着腿，双手抱着膝盖，一连几个小时一动不动地坐着——想心事；可是说来奇怪，他从不曾灰心绝望，也不曾怨恨自己的命。他深信自己眼下的遭遇，是在补赎一桩死罪——在他看来，未经宗教婚礼便与一个女人共同生活，便是一桩死罪——而且他绝对相信，这罪一赎完，他的清白总有一天会大白于天下，他会获得自由。与此同时，他虽然不绝望，却痛苦万分，在神经质的期待中挨着一天天、一钟头一钟头、一分一秒，盼着一场始终不来的变化，同时又受着噬人乡愁的煎熬。就这样，一天又一天，一时又一时，一刻又一刻，他在思念中守着乔凡娜和孩子过日子，把茅屋生活、把他的往昔、把一度属于他的幸福中每一件鸡毛蒜皮的小事，都分毫不差地回想起来。此外，除了自身的痛苦，他一想到乔凡娜正在受的罪，就更加难受：时不时会有一阵炽烈的柔情攫住他——那柔情远更多是为了她，而不是为了孩子——把他从惯常的郁郁沉思中惊起；于是他一跃而起，大步来回走动——两步，至多三步，就到了对面的墙前——到了墙边他便停住，把整个人朝墙上扑去，用头去磕墙，仿佛要把脑浆撞出来似的。这是他绝望到极点的时刻。

===Original===
Hope always returned, however, and then he would begin to weave fantastic dreams of an immediate and romantic restoration to freedom, and the guard never entered his cell that his heart did not begin to beat violently, fancying that he was the bearer of some joyful tidings.

===Chinese===
然而希望总会回来；这时他就开始编织种种离奇的梦，梦见自己转眼间便带着传奇意味地恢复了自由；每当看守走进牢房，他的心总怦怦狂跳，以为对方是来报什么喜讯的。

===Original===
Sometimes he played *morra* with himself, and he cared so much whether he lost or won that he would laugh aloud like a child. At other times he would sit for hours looking at his outstretched palm, imagining that it was a plain divided into *tancas*, with walls, rivers, trees, herds of cattle, and shepherds; and weaving stories about them all, full of exciting adventures. And sometimes he prayed, counting on his fingers, and repeating the lauds aloud, trying even to improvise new verses. In this way it came about that he actually did compose a laud of four strophes, dedicated to St. Costantino, in which the saint’s aid was particularly invoked in behalf of all prisoners wrongfully condemned. The refrain ran:

===Chinese===
他有时独自玩*莫拉*（morra）猜拳，还十分计较输赢，赢了输了都会像孩子似的放声大笑。有时他会一连几个小时坐着端详自己摊开的手掌，把它想象成一片用围墙分割成一块块*tanca*的平原，有墙垣、河流、树木、牛群和牧人；他给这一切编故事，故事里满是惊心动魄的奇遇。有时他祈祷，扳着手指计数，高声念诵圣赞歌，甚至还试着即兴编出新的诗句。就这样，他居然真的编出了一首四节的赞歌，献给圣科斯坦蒂诺，歌中特别祈求圣人庇佑所有蒙冤定罪的囚徒。叠句是这样唱的：

===Original===
> “Saint Costantino, we implore thee
 

 For thy condemned innocent!”

===Chinese===
> “圣科斯坦蒂诺，我们恳求你，
 

 垂怜你那蒙冤定罪的人！”

===Original===
The composing of this laud completely occupied him for many days, and made him, for the time being, almost happy. When it was finished he was wild with joy, but instantly an overpowering desire to tell someone about it seized him; whom was there, though, to tell? The guard was a little Neapolitan; bald, clean-shaven, with a flat, snub nose like that of a skeleton; he talked to him sometimes, but he was not sufficiently intelligent to understand the laud; then there were the other prisoners whom he saw during the exercise hour, but to them he was not allowed to speak; finally he bethought him of the chaplain, and asked to confess in order that he might have the opportunity to repeat the laud to him. The chaplain was a Northerner, a young man, tall and lean, with quick, nervous movements, and great flashing black eyes filled with intelligence.
